- **Ondra Kelb counts it twice, with his left hand, and finds that it balances and that nobody has done anything wrong**, and says the sentence he said in the seed month and has been ashamed of since, and then says the reason the relief is worse the second time: **it is not mine; Ondra Kelb is not a party to a thing in a shed in Tallow Ward.** He asks for a column to put that in. **Sedge says there is no column and is not cutting a seventh, because a seventh is a column and a column gets a name and a name gets a heading. He is not asked about the hold and his left hand is a nine-year-old's and is not his.**
- **The person who does not know what to say (Ch 87) is a man of about fifty with a bad cough** whose name is on the fifty-two and who says: *that is my name, I did not sign anything, my wife signed it, she is at the water tank twice a day and is fifty-one and cannot get up the causeway in this cold*, and who was told his own name out loud in his own doorway in the seed month by a person in a good coat with a book. **The register's answer, from the yard's own rules and given by Marta Brel from the step of the shed: the second line takes his words, the fourth line is for anything anybody else has to say, and the two are printed one above the other and are not joined.** He is not satisfied and the chapter does not say that he is. **A bundle may not be entered as one line, and a line is not always one person, and nobody solves it.**
- **Ch 90, the twenty-ninth, the fourth and last morning the register is open to look.** It is **not** the first morning; **Ch 95 is where the register goes live, and Ch 100 is the first morning it is live, and a later writer must not confuse the fourth morning here with the first morning there.** Forty-one lines in forty-one hands on the fourth morning, from one on the twenty-first.
- **The person who finds herself and does not know what to do about it is Ilma Tresch, forty-one.** **Her facts as Ch 90 now states them, which are the repaired ones: she drew one of the eleven trays in the fourth week of the seed month, filled cones and carried two sacks, did the eleven days in the growth month on ground that had already failed, and has had a cough since the ninth of the seed month which is now in the small of her back; the certificate is Sera Holt's and it covers four of the days, and it went to the clinic board, and the board entered it as a cost, and the district's answer to a cost is a rate.** *(Ch 90 used to say she did all eleven days and that a doctor wrote her out of four of the eleven in one sentence, and gave the cough a second origin; Ch 73:9/43 and Ch 80:55 are the authority and Ch 80:59 is the authority for the four.)* **The Share entered its own return-in-kind rule on the twenty-second, in Fisk's own hand, in four lines, read out and not improved, with *a seed does not fail because of a schedule* asked for on the fourth line.** Her ground is the sixth of the eleven. **Her line is right, and there is still no roll in this city with her figure on it, no office that holds it and no way to produce a copy of a thing nobody has**, and now there is a room in which any person may find it and have it read out for nothing. **She came to find out whether her own line was right, and it is, and she has not got the faintest idea what to do about it, and she says it to the room because there is nobody in it she could say it to — the man at the counter cannot enter anything, the collector is a member of the Share and not of her household, the boy goes home to his mother — and she asks nobody to stop her and nobody to thank her.** **The piece of sack behind her line was written on the third day of the harvest month and is in Fisk's own house, not in a building; the Share's own entry in the register went in on the twenty-second of the harvest month, which is a different day, and the two must not be conflated.**
- **Wenna Drel at the levy trestle (Ch 90) is the batch's last image of a person the rules cannot reach.** She is sixteen, she is on the roll because her mother is, **she is not a member, she is a person**, the cap is her mother's, there is no form on which anybody asks her, **and she asks for it to be written down that she said it and that nobody asked her whether she minded, and nobody asks her.** Bessok Tan puts the board down, refuses to write her name, and sends her to read her mother's ground instead. **She stands four feet off the counter in a room of strangers for a quarter of an hour, asks nothing, reads nothing, and goes home. Nobody follows her and nobody writes anything down.**
- **The register keeps no total** and the yard said out loud in the seed month why. **Nobody in Tallow Ward has ever been able to find out how many people were standing in a lane outside a shed, and this is the first thing the room is good at and the only one anybody could pay for.**
- **The costs the register has already incurred and has not paid:** the queue takes the road and the yard's water did not come down the Ropewalk standpipe on the fourth morning, because **one of the two people who carry the yard's water stood in the lane with two pails for four minutes and then carried them back up the lane and did not come down again that day**; **about nine people stopped coming in the first fortnight and Pike says he has no idea which nine and neither does the room**; a rumour went forty feet along the lane that the register is going to make credit safe, and was stopped by a man who had been in the seed house; **and the form has a fifth line for the hours, empty because the hours are nobody's paper, and a sixth line that is empty because there is no box for the days.** The girl of sixteen was told to expect none of it and came back.

### Marek, at the end of Movement IV

- **He came into the movement with the sentence he said out loud in a witness room on the twentieth of the growth month, and the first thing the batch establishes is that it stopped nothing and the second is that he stayed useful anyway.** Six offices refused his proposal in six days and none of them was unkind and one of them cost him something.
- **He is given, by other people and by no form, the standing duty of a man who reads the register out. IT IS NOW ON THE PAGE.** **Ch 82 had asserted it in four later chapters and in every state file without ever granting it, and the batch 0004 review is what caught that the volume's hinge was happening off the page. Ch 82 now carries the grant in full**, at the end of the chapter, from Kern on the step of the tank, at about the sixth bell, in about a hundred and forty people's hearing and **not voted on either time**: *There is a man standing at the end of this yard. He has been in it five weeks and he has not written one word of any of it down and he has not asked one of you a single question, and I have watched him not do it every day…* — **the register is read out by Marek Vale, four hours a day on whatever days it opens, in the words the person gave it in, and he stops when somebody says *that is not what I said*, and if he does not stop he is a man in a good coat and not a reader, and anybody in the yard is to say so to his face and in front of other people; and he cannot enter one word of it, and that is the form and not a punishment.** It is **not a condition, not a favour, and there is no paper of it anywhere**, and one man says *you cannot give a man a standing* and Kern answers that the yard can give him the four hours and the road and the words and nobody in this city can take one of the three off him. **Marek says thank you and Sanne Droll says *do not*, and nobody thanks anybody, which the chapter calls the fourth condition and which is not a condition.** Ch 88 then records it: it is **not in the finding**, and Harrow writes it into a minute of a board of five in his own hand with **no name at the foot of the paragraph at all**, and says why: a Crown March representative has no business granting standing to a man who has none. **The form has no box for him. He may not enter a line in the register and he does not try to. He has no office, no post, no pay and no right of entry at the end of the batch, and he is the only thing in the room that works.**
- **Reading is not entering, and the posting desk's duty clerk says it** — sixty in the year, twenty-two years in the copy room — and adds the half nobody had thought of: in a year somebody will ask him whether he checked the figures, he will have to say no, they will be right not to like it, **and there is nobody at that counter who checks except a yard's second person who is not a reader and not a keeper**, whom she has never met.
- **The flat board is put down on the trestle under the form on the twenty-ninth (Ch 90),** where anybody can lift it off, and nobody ever does. **It was four ruled sheets of the Share's instrument and schedule, a copy and not an instrument and not evidence, entered nowhere, and the only thing in the basin that cannot be produced in any room by anybody, and he made it.** He has never been able to say clearly why he did it.
- **He has no hold, no panel, no office, no form, no work and no room, and he did not get a card, a post, a wage, a vote or an office out of the card (Ch 89).** **He still has the little book of promises to his mother and the recertification list of twenty-two files with a pencil line on the back of it, and no figure exists anywhere for how many of the twenty-two files he finished and none may be invented.**

### Ovid Merrow, at the end of Movement IV — locked

- **He ends the movement more useful to Rainmarket than he began it and is refuted by nothing.** He gave the count of what a reading is worth and what not knowing costs, in a shed, in front of nine people, against his own interest. He gave Pike's the standing of a market reason and entered his own house's refusal in public. **He was not convicted, no licence is withdrawn, the Compact is not dissolved, Ferrow does not approve, and he does not come past a step.**
- **The card is spent and it was never a bribe (Ch 89).** He enters it himself, on a bench, in the open: a figure of his, given to a man with no office on the fifth of the seed month, more than the hall pays, not for a post, not for a vote, for one document and one reading of it, refused on a causeway in four seconds. **What it bought:** the second eye on the Share's instrument had to be a counter-desk clerk who had never been to Rainmarket and did not know what a counterweight was, and who read the fourth clause out loud in a shed **badly, on purpose, and told the room he was doing it badly on purpose and why** — *and that is the whole of the risk in this basin this year, and there is a room at the Tallow Gate because a man with no office could not be paid.* He asks for it minuted that he offered it and it was refused. **Nobody thanks him and Marek gets nothing.**
- **He says, in a shed, to about nine people at a door, that the thing that is going to beat him is not a register: it is a yard's method with a name on it**, and that a yard is not a person and he could not buy it off a bench in front of him, **and that he is entitled to be annoyed that the price of an explanation is going to nothing and entitled to say so in a lane, and is not going to be a martyr about it, because he built the first one.** **He is about to lose a monopoly he is entitled to: the right to be the only person in the basin who explains a piece of paper. Ch 95 is where the licence goes.**
- **What he does not have and did not learn:** **there is no number for how many people are in his claims and there never has been, and there is no form in this kingdom in which anybody may be given it** — *a line is a household, not a person; fifty-two households is not fifty-two people; forty of forty-one in a shed is not a majority; and he has been selling lines for nine years and calling them claims.* **The two figures he will stand behind are fifty-two lines and thirty-eight answered and fourteen did not out of fifty-two, and the second is unchanged from Ch 69.** He does not know what the marks are, has not been told the page has been copied, has not mentioned the oldest page, and **did not learn anything about the counterseal in Ch 85 — he is not in that chapter and the third hand is not mentioned to him.**
- **The seventh column in the low-ground lane shed is still bare at the head of it, as it has been since the twentieth of the seed month, which is the day a board painter and a signwriter were sent home and the day the column was left without a name over it, and nothing in Movement IV names it or proposes a heading for it.** A board painter and a signwriter were sent home on the twentieth of the seed month and are not in this batch. **This line previously gave the twentieth of the seed month and the twenty-second in the same sentence, and the chapters support the twentieth: Ch 69 dates the two men sent home to the twentieth, and Ch 89 says the seventh "was bare at the head of it and had been since the day the painters were sent home". The twenty-second is the day the register opened, not the day the column lost its heading.**

### Halvard Sunn and the counterseal, and the third instrument (Ch 85) — the Volume 03 plant, and it is planted and not overplayed

- **A Tide Reckoning mark names four things** — a month in a calendar that is not yours, a tide phase, a harbor seal, a translator — **and it is not a warranty about a figure, and a notary's standing is a list at a harbour which would say only *yes*.** **An audit in this city goes as far as the seal and stops, and the seal is the whole of the paper's portability, and the register cannot be read outside the basin and cannot be read in the next street.** **A harbor notary is not the Saltglass Harbor Council and the Council has not been named.**
- **Sunn refuses to warrant a figure, again, and it costs him something this time and he states the cost in his own rooms:** a house at home will ask in a month whether Morrowfen paper is being read outside Morrowfen, and he will have to write *I do not know*, and a notary who writes that is a notary they will not need in a year, and he has two rooms on a lease charged in Crown March money and a press that is not his and no second office in this city. **He has refused twice in this volume, on the board and on the book, and this is the third and it is the first about his own standing.**
- **THE PLANT IS THREE INSTRUMENTS AND TWO SENTENCES.** **The counterseal is on the Compact's bundle of nine sheets and a schedule of fifty-two lines; it is on the board over the door of the low-ground lane shed; and it is on a Red Uplands grain contract — forty-one acres off the second terrace is not in it, it is a haulier's paper for barley brought down by **Petar Duss**, a drayman, with a view to selling it at a harbour, and it has nothing whatever to do with the Share, the low ground, the flood quarters, the fourth clause or the ninth month.** The hand is the same hand. **Marek has now seen it three times, has written nothing down, and has told nobody, deliberately, twice, and the chapter says why: two people in this city would have carried it into a room inside the hour and neither of them could have said which of the three it was about.** **Nobody in Morrowfen can read one, none of the three can be audited, and the volume's next question — why do signatures get copied across unrelated contracts — is Volume 03's and is answered there.**
- **Sunn says out loud, in that room, on that day, before either of them asks the other, that he does not know what the marks are, that he has not known for nine years, and that the reason he stopped asking is that he makes a very good living out of a mark nobody in this basin can check.** **He must not learn anything further in this volume, and nobody in Morrowfen knows who A-0 is.**

### Oren Pike, at the end of Movement IV

- **The first person in the basin to put his own house's figures into a public register, and he gives them for a reason he states without shame and keeps his profit**, and the record of the reason is kept in his own words in front of his own floor. **He is not forgiven and is not written as a good man.** He is honest about what it bought: he had to go to a counter-desk clerk who did not know what a counterweight was. **He is also honest about what it will cost: he does not have his margin figure and will not invent one in front of eleven men, and he says the difference between the price of a reading and the worth of a room is the whole of his trade and he has never been asked for the second one.** He is not a lender, and no second lender enters the batch. **He prices the levy and the price is in the finding.**
- **HOW THE HUNDRED AND FORTY IS DERIVED — CORRECTED at the batch 0004 review.** The figure is unchanged and is still one hundred and forty a season and a little over two pounds a head on sixty-one households, **but the old derivation was arithmetically impossible**: four a week over a twelve-week season is forty-eight, and 140 ÷ 4 is thirty-five weeks, and Ch 77 fixes a season at twelve weeks. Ch 83 now derives it **the way a factor actually derives a levy figure, which is from what the paying body can carry and not from what the work costs**: *I asked what a household on the low ground can carry for a room it does not use, without anybody having to be asked a second question, and it is a little over two pounds, and I have had that figure off a floor for nineteen years and not out of my own head. I put it against sixty-one lines and it comes to about a hundred and forty.* **He now says out loud that four a week and a hundred and forty a season do not agree with each other and that he did not get one out of the other**, which is why the eleven men on the slate do not catch him out. **Ch 88 was corrected to match: a season of that is *not* a hundred and forty, and the two pounds is where it started and the hundred and forty is what it comes to.** A later batch may not re-derive the figure from four a week.

### Bel Harrow, the board and term four — locked

- **A table of five with the Trust's seat empty for the fourth time**, *in the ordinary course* still in quotation marks, Harrow serving the minutes in his own hand with no clerk, and no date for anything. **The four terms are confirmed in eleven minutes and have been used three times.** **Term four — a member who dissents may have the grounds written in her own words, and the duty to write them is the board's and not the member's — does the movement's work about money, and it does it by producing the first definition of a levy in the basin and the cap that comes with it.** **Vendl has been a member of a mutual for nineteen years, has never used it for anything, and says so before she uses it.** The Trust will not fill a chair and the Fund cannot pay a sitting and a ward cannot be assessed a rate at all. **All three are true and none of them is resolved.**

### New names on the page in this batch, and the ones that are not

- **Sedge** appears properly in this batch, as the man who cuts the form, and **still has no service length anywhere and may not be given one.** **Petar Duss** is the drayman of Ch 74 and is here once, with a grain contract and a question he does not understand the answer to; **he is not a source, not a witness and not a villager, and he is not told anything.** **The posting desk's duty clerk** is given an age — **sixty in the year**, with her established twenty-two years in the copy room — and no other new fact. **The four lines were written by a hand that is not named in the batch and may not be named.**
- **No new service lengths.** **Nineteen years still belongs to Vendl, Grale, Ostrowsky, Harrow and Vey and to nobody else.** Sedge has none; Sunn has four years in this city and none in an office; Sarn eleven; Krela twenty-nine; Hesper, Senn and Rezek twenty-two; Holt twenty-six; Vahl six; Bessok Tan four as secretary; Duss is not given one; Malnar has none; Benda Horvat is dead; Stasek is eighty-one. **Signe Alve is four a week and has no other figure in the batch.**
- **Name traps still live.** **Ostrowsky** the settlement-desk clerk and the **Ostrow** family of Tallow Ward are still unrelated and are not mentioned once in this batch. **Sedge and Sarn** are different people. **Sera Holt** the physician is not mentioned once in this batch and her hidden clinic loan is Volume 12's. **Milan Vray** the yard-man, **Teodor Lask** the nineteen-year-old reader, **Sanne Droll** the rope-maker, **Aldis Kern**, **Marta Brel** forty-six, **Ilma Tresch** forty-one, **Wenna Drel** sixteen, **Bessok Tan** twenty-nine, **Mirren Fisk** thirty-four, **Mirren Fisk's eleven trays and the bench**, **Sabina Krela** is not in this batch, **Tam Rell** fourteen, **Petar Duss**, **Josip Malnar** forty-nine, **Halda Brnec** is not in this batch.

### Ledger Sight and Boundary Hold in this batch: zero and zero

**No Ledger Sight panel is used in Chapters 81–90. Zero.** The budget was at most one with zero as the target and it is met at zero. **No Boundary Hold is used, wished for, hinted at or mentioned, and the founding standing hold is not referred to at all.** **Marek's forearm is not described, referred to or looked at anywhere in the ten chapters, and no second person decodes either entry in the vault book on the second floor.** **The two holds of Movement III are not re-run as a scene and the lesson is not restated as a speech; it is on the page in Ch 81 in a form of words, and what Ch 81 does with it is put it in a box the yard had already refused.** Tamsin Vale is not mentioned and is asked nothing. **The second cost of the power is paid entirely in usefulness and in days:** a yard that will not give him its method, five correct refusals in six days, a flat board put down on a trestle under a form, a duty given by a yard that no form carries, and a room where about sixty people stand in a lane and one of them is a man with no office who can only read.

### There is no villain in this batch and none was manufactured

**Lund, Vey, Vendl, Sedge, Harrow, Kern, Vray, Droll, Lask, Fisk, Tan, Brel, Pike, Sunn, Merrow, Duss, Malnar, Alve and the yard itself all act correctly and all cost the reader something.** Sedge offers the one thing that would work and is refused. Vey is right. Vendl gives away the most useful thing in the chapter for nothing. Pike is right and keeps his profit. Merrow is right and is about to lose a licence he is entitled to. **The only criminal available is a rule that puts a cost in a box with no person in it, and this batch adds three more of them: a form whose second line has one word in it and needs three people; a levy whose cap produces a member who has not been asked; and a register whose rule excludes the paper it was built to beat.**

### Deliberate divergences and notes for the reviewer

- **Length, counted from the files with the title line included: 30,510 words across ten chapters, and the batch is OVER the 24,000–28,000 plan by 2,510.** The distribution is **Ch 81 3,562 · Ch 82 3,130 · Ch 83 3,015 · Ch 84 3,192 · Ch 85 2,390 · Ch 86 2,933 · Ch 87 2,540 · Ch 88 2,923 · Ch 89 3,336 · Ch 90 3,489.** The shortest is 2,390 and the longest is 3,562. **Eight chapters are above 2,800 and one is below 2,400, and no chapter is under 2,300.** **This is the batch's overrun, it is recorded rather than rounded down, and at 30,510 it is the largest batch in the volume, larger than batch 0003's 30,279 — the earlier version of this line said the opposite, and said 29,104, and nine of its ten per-chapter figures were stale, Ch 81's being the one that was already right. The stale figures had a cause: the batch 0004 review-fix pass rewrote sentences in nine of the ten chapters, Chapters 82 to 90 among them, and the counts were never retaken, so the numbers described the chapters as they stood before their own review. Retake every count after a fix pass, not before it.** The reason is not padding: **Ch 81 carries five offices and a yard and each refusal has to be a different correct sentence, and Ch 90 is the movement's queue and carries the levy trestle, a girl at a trestle, the Share's own entry and the woman who finds herself, and the brief forbids cutting a complete scene to hit a number.** The files were trimmed repeatedly during drafting and **the counts above were taken from the files on 2026-09-26, after the batch 0004 review fix and after the batch 0005 review, and they are the figures to use.**
- **Ten engines and no long table.** A yard and a refused proposal; a yard, a sheet with no name on it and a vote; a counting floor at the second bell with a call and four seconds of silence; two days of argument in a yard and a seed house and a form being cut by a man who is not sure of it; a notary's two rooms and a mark; a yard trying to enter its own best paper and failing; a shed with a queue and a man who does not know what to say; a hearing room built for thirty with nine in it and a dissent written out; a shed and a boy of fourteen and three questions; a lane at the fourth bell and a counter. **No chapter's load-bearing event is a document read out to a room. Ch 87 is the volume's third practice chapter and the reading of Pike's six lines is not the chapter: the chapter is the man of fifty who does not know what to say, and the room is the room and the argument, and the eleven lines from Ch 80 are deliberately not read out again.** No chapter ends on a figure or on a notice spoken to a crowd. Ch 82 ends on two people in a lane and a tank; Ch 84 ends on a woman on a posting desk and a tray; Ch 88 ends on a Chair alone with a hat on and a paragraph with no name at the foot of it; Ch 90 ends on a lane filling up and a first line read out four times.
- **Panel budget: ZERO Ledger Sight panels in ten chapters.** **No Boundary Hold. The founding standing hold is not used, offered, wished for or hinted at. Marek's forearm is not described, referred to or looked at. Tamsin is asked nothing and is not mentioned.**
- **The volume's guardrails are intact across Chapters 81–90.** Nobody in Morrowfen knows who A-0 is and the mark is not mentioned once. **Merrow learned nothing:** he does not mention the oldest page, does not know what the marks are, is not in Ch 85, and has not been told the page has been copied. **The founding standing hold is not used, offered, wished for or hinted at. The ward roll naming district four and the name Kallow was not mentioned once. Pella Rusk's surety cell is untouched. The clerk of record is not advanced. Tamsin is asked nothing.** **No Choir, no military body, no regional political crisis, no Crown of Continuation, no second lender and no Saltglass Harbor Council; a harbor notary is not the Council, and Pike is a factor and not a lender.** **No office was closed, no licence withdrawn and nobody was convicted, and the register does not look like a fix: its rule excludes the Compact's paper, it has an empty fifth line, its sixth line is permanently empty, it keeps no total, it has already stopped the yard's water once and lost nine people in a fortnight, and a hungry man can still sign anything anywhere.**
- **Figures the batch creates and a later batch must not break:** **two a reading, sixty to ninety a week, four a week, one hundred and forty a season, a little over two pounds a head**; **about a hundred and sixty a year for the man who tells Merrow what a paper is**; **four shillings** for a counterseal on a haulier's contract; **forty-one lines in forty-one hands in the register, of which two are named** (Pike's house on the twenty-first, the Share's return-in-kind rule on the twenty-second, the sixth line of the eleven being Ilma Tresch's ground); **about nine people who stopped coming**; **about nine people who left Fisk's reading**; **the four and five and five and five and six of the ward's four rules as printed on the sheet of the ninth.** **Eleven of sixty-one trays failed and is a count of trays and not a count of people and no chapter may turn it into a fraction. Forty of forty-one in a shed is not a majority and Merrow says so in Ch 89. Thirty-eight answered and fourteen did not out of fifty-two in Merrow's register is unchanged.**
- **The chain does not move in this batch and must not.** **The ninth link is collectable and uncollected, the arrival is Movement V's, and nobody in Morrowfen may find out before Chapter 93 that Tallow Ward is the end of it.** **The chain is not mentioned once in Chapters 81–90 except in Ch 83 and Ch 88, and in neither of those it is traced:** Ch 83 has Josip Malnar say what his four hundred and ten pounds is and ask that the district not be called him, which is the fourth link and a man who has already been told he is the fourth, and **Ch 88's clock paragraph names the ninth month, the water office's letter, the school and the empty seat and does not say what a chain is.** The batch ends with the ninth month almost on top of them, a cut that is not a date, a lamp that has not come off the ground, and a rule about a person who cannot be served with a notice.
- **`state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and untouched. The batch summary lives in `state/current.md` because no separate batch-summary file exists in the repository layout.**
- **A self-reported pass in this section is not evidence.** The findings above were produced by reading the ten finished chapters back and grepping them, and the counts were taken from the files after the last edit. **A reviewer should re-read before treating any of it as closed** — and the batch 0003 review is the proof of that sentence rather than a contradiction of it, because it found nine prose defects and four false claims in this repository's own artefacts.

### Batch 0004 review repairs — what was changed on the page and what was wrong before

**The batch 0004 review returned ten blocking findings and seven smaller ones and every one of them has been acted on in the chapters, not papered over in the state files. What follows is the audit trail, so that the next writer and the next reviewer do not have to re-derive it. No plot, no character motive, no date in the batch's own calendar, and no volume plan was changed.**

1. **The card was in the wrong coat (Ch 89).** Ch 70:111 and Ch 80:141 both put Merrow's card on Marek — *a card in his coat*, *a card in his coat he had not used and will not use* — and Ch 89 had Merrow pull it out of **his own** inside pocket with no handover anywhere. **Fixed on the page: Ch 89 now has Merrow hold out his hand and say *the card*, Marek take it out of the inside pocket of the coat it has been in since the fifth of the seed month and put it on the bench, and Merrow give it back four seconds later without explaining either.** The custody is unbroken and the beat is stronger, because the card ends the chapter back in Marek's hands.
2. **Marek was not in Ch 89 at all** (one grep hit, the last paragraph). **Fixed: he has an arrival and a reason** — a boy of about nine carries a four-word message up to the Gate at the second bell, *come down and stand there*, in a factor's hand, paid a penny, and Marek comes down the low-ground road on foot in the rain at the fifth bell and tells nobody why, *which is a thing a man with no office can do and is one of about four things he can still do*. **He also has a line now, and it is the only one he says in the chapter:** the room at the Tallow Gate is a yard's doing and not a factor's, and he would rather Merrow wrote that down than the other one.
3. **Ch 89:85 said Merrow's register was *forty-nine days old*, which is impossible** (the twenty-second of the seed month to the twenty-sixth of the harvest month is months, and the month lengths between are fixed nowhere). **Fixed: the sentence now says *Mine has been open since the twenty-second of the seed month*** — an ordinal, not a count, which is the only form the calendar can support. The root cause was this file's own *The harvest month opens MR 414*, corrected above.
4. **Ch 90:73 gave the piece of sack the wrong date** — *the twenty-second of the seed month*, which is the date Merrow's register opened. **The sack was written on the third day of the harvest month** (Ch 80:7, and Fisk's *since the first of this month*). Fixed. Ch 90:75's *on the twenty-second of this month* for the Share's entry is a different day and is correct.
5. **Marta Brel's husband (Ch 84:13) was alive and dead in one sentence**, and contradicted Ch 76, where the form was *filled in her husband's hand* and the settlement was *eleven days of her husband's labour*. **Fixed: he is alive, he says the figure out loud for her at the counter in about four seconds, he works the north road and there are weeks when he is not in this city, and there is nobody else.** The chapter's turn — that she needs a *rule* and not a husband — is now actually earned, because the husband is a real and insufficient answer.
6. **Ilma Tresch (Ch 90:71) contradicted herself twice in one sentence** and lost her physician. **Fixed:** *a cough since the ninth of the seed month* (Ch 80:55, not *came in with the flood water*), **Sera Holt named**, and *did the eleven days in the growth month* rather than *all eleven days … and a doctor wrote her out of four of the eleven days*.
7. **The form's fifth line.** Ch 84:107 names it on the page — *Hours, which are four a day* — and Ch 87:105 and Ch 90:123 then said nobody could say what it was for, and four state artefacts had inherited the lie. **Fixed in all three places and in the state files: the fifth line is the hours, and it is empty because the hours are nobody's paper.** See the *The six lines* entry above.
8. **The hundred and forty was not derivable.** See the Oren Pike entry above. The figure is unchanged; the derivation is now a factor's derivation.
9. **The yard's standing to read the register was asserted in four chapters and in every state file and granted nowhere.** See *Marek, at the end of Movement IV* above — **it is now on the page in Ch 82**, which is what Harrow's minute, the posting desk clerk's conditional, and Ch 90:113 all assumed.
10. **Sunn's post (Ch 85) was three different lengths.** He had been four years in the city, in the second year of a four-year post, and paid by a house for four years. **Fixed: the fourth and last year of a four-year post**, which agrees with the opening sentence and with the last line of the chapter.

**The seven smaller findings, all fixed in the prose:** the *fourth time in five weeks* in Ch 90 (which collided with four mornings inside eight days) is now *the fourth time since the twenty-first*; Ch 90:9's invented *exactly as a grain factor had said in a shed on the low-ground road a fortnight before* is gone, and the queue that sorts itself by nothing is now explained by the yard's own Ch 84 rule that **the register has no list**; Ch 88:33's *in a lane on the twelfth* is now *on a counting floor on the twelfth* with about ninety people, which is Ch 83; Ch 90:51 and :57 now agree — the girl is on her **mother's line**, and the roll has no place for a girl **who is on her mother's line because her mother is** — and *the fourth of hers* is now *the fourth from the end of the board*, per Ch 52:73; Ch 90's *one of them* for the water carrier is correct in the prose and **the state files were wrong** and now read *one of the two people who carry the yard's water*; **Kern's *And I wrote it* in Ch 86 is replaced with a decision, not a hedge** — he claims the sentence and not the hand, and says he does not know who held the pen and that this yard is not going to find out, which is the only reason the sentence is any good, and which agrees with Ch 82, with the same paragraph nine lines earlier, and with the standing canon that the pen is never named; Ch 86:115's *a hundred and forty people who have been examined this season* was borrowing Tallow Ward's yard figure and is now *the women I have examined this season, of whom there are a great many*; and Ch 90:65's *went out of the room twice* while she stands there once is now **once**, which leaves the *once before, at a gate in the rains* as the only second thing in the sentence.

**One thing a repair pass nearly got wrong, recorded so nobody gets it wrong next time: *a hundred and forty people* in Merrow's lane is canon and was not touched.** Ch 69:35 has Ovid Merrow explain the bare column *on the twenty-second, in front of about a hundred and forty people*, and Ch 69:47 has him use the count himself — so the figure is Merrow's own, at his own door, and not a borrowing of Tallow Ward's yard. Ch 89 keeps it and only had its **date** corrected, from *the second of the seed month* to *the twenty-second of the seed month*, which is the day the bench was painted and the register opened. The one place the borrowing was real is Ch 86:115, where Kade walks to the low ground to look at people she has examined, and that is the one that was changed.

**Ch 89:9 and Ch 80:107.** Merrow promised, on the page on the third of the harvest month, that on the twenty-second **his own** register would carry a line for each of the eleven households the Share had written to. **That promise is now paid on the page: the bench has fifty-two lines and then eleven more, the sixth column went up on the nineteenth of the growth month for the fifty-two letters, the eleventh of the eleven went on on the twenty-second at the fourth bell in eleven hands and not one of them the factor's, and Merrow says so in the shed** — sixty-three lines, the other eleven off a piece of sack with nothing at the head of it that Fisk has in her own house and will not give to anybody. **And the Gate shed's age is corrected: it opened on the twenty-first of the harvest month, so on the twenty-sixth it has been open five days, not two, and it has not got one line in it.** `open-threads.md` recorded the eleven households as a fact before it was on the page; it is now both.

**Two state-file errors found on the same pass and corrected here:** `current.md` described Ch 84 as *a yard, with no exchange*, and Ch 84 is a direct exchange in which the beat is that Marek does not thank Kade; and `current.md` attributed the count of a reading — *two a reading, sixty to ninety a week, two thirds houses* — to Merrow, when it is Pike's, from his counting floor in Ch 83, and Merrow's figure in Ch 89 is the hundred and sixty a year for a clerk whose name he does not know. The figures themselves are unchanged and remain locked.

**The calendar note above is the one to read before dating anything.** The harvest month's length is still not dated in the batch and must not be dated by batch 0005, and the three locked distance figures stand: Ch 80 *just under four weeks off*, Ch 81 *short of a month away*, Ch 90 *almost on top of them* with no number. **A batch 0005 writer may not compute a day count backwards from the ninth month across a month whose length is not in the manuscript, and may not say a month is a stated number of days old.**
