## VOLUME 02, BATCH 0005 (Chapters 91–100) — "What the Paper Is Worth" — THE VOLUME'S CLOSE

Prose is authoritative. This is **the fifth and last of the five batches in Volume 02.** Chapters 1–90 are not touched. `outline/volume-02.md` was not edited. **Movement V is complete and the volume is closed.**

**The rule from the previous four sections carries: every clause below is a quotation or a note about a quotation, and a note that cannot be pointed at a line does not go in. Treat every verification sentence here as unproven until the chapter has been re-read.**

### The calendar, and what the batch moved

**Batch dates: the second (Ch 91), the fourth (Ch 92), the sixth (Ch 93), the seventh (Ch 94), the ninth (Ch 95), the twelfth (Ch 96), the twelfth to the fourteenth (Ch 97), the fifteenth (Ch 98), the seventeenth (Ch 99) and the eighteenth (Ch 100) of the ninth month, MR 413.** The batch opens with the ninth month named and closes on the first morning of the live register. **The harvest month's length is not dated anywhere in Chapters 91–100 and is not dated here.** Ch 90's *almost on top of them* is honoured by Ch 91's *The ninth month came in on a wet morning*; no distance figure is given in the batch and no batch sentence contradicts *just under four weeks off* (Ch 80), *short of a month away* (Ch 81) or *almost on top of them* (Ch 90).

- **Weekdays: the batch states none as a claim about a day.** A correctly-run grep over Chapters 91–100, excluding *Crown March* as a street and a district, returns **nothing at all** for any real-world month name and **nothing at all** for any weekday. **This is the first batch in the volume with a clean return on both, and the reason is that two errors were caught and fixed during drafting**: Ch 96 had written *on a Tuesday he is not going to describe to anybody* and Ch 93 had written *if I go in there on Monday* — one a claim about a day, one a claim about a day, neither correct, both now *on an ordinary morning of the second month* and *on the morning of the market*.
- **Bells** (inherited, unchanged): day bells first to sixth, the sixth when the copy room shuts, the seventh in the evening, the eighth struck in the morning. **First through seventh are all used across the ten chapters. There is no ninth bell and none is used.**
- **The growth month is twenty days long and is behind us** (Ch 97 refers to it twice, both times as a past season). **The third month is used three times and is never dated for its length** — it is the month the office supplies a lamp (Ch 93), the month the ward's lamp comes back, and the month a crew's three seasons of eleven days end (Ch 99's man says the span will be up "in November" in Volume 01 and says nothing about it here).

### The ninth month's demand, and what a district account is (Ch 91) — locked

- **The first letter (Ch 51) said *kindly exhibit the line and advise the district.* The ninth month's letter says the district named is to be charged and the charge entered, and the office will wait until the close of the month.** A young man of about twenty-six carries it up Bell Stair on foot in a leather tube and apologises for the tube; he is told the word aloud and he does not understand any of it and **is not a party to anything and is never named again.**
- **A district account is defined on the page for the first time and the definition is a fact, not a complaint:** it is a page, kept in a book, on a shelf in the levy office, and **it shows what is owed *to* a district**; money is only a thing that happens when an office writes a line in a book saying a claim has been dealt with, and then the claim is off the page, **because a claim that has been paid is not owed.** The standing carry is a line entered once a year since before the flood years and never applied, and a district's name beside a number turns a number into a debt. **What a yard pays at the ordinary wage is drawn from the households and never comes through this account — the recited reserve does not touch the households.**
- **Why the demand cannot be answered by anybody (Ch 91), and this is the volume's institutional spine in one sentence:** applying an unapplied sum requires holding the district account to its presented figure, **and holding a district account to its presented figure is a cut, and the water office is permitted one such order and holds no copy of it, and the officer who enters it is Grale.** He will not do a fifth cut on a sheet that arrived in a tube, and he says the arithmetic out loud: a second cut in one year, and then the next one is a request from a man in a good coat and there will be no answer to it, **and I will be the man who made the first two in one year and there will be no third.**
- **Marek finds nine names on the Tallow account that are not on the Gate tank's own count, under a line headed with a kind and not a place and not a person, and writes two words on the back of the recertification list — *a kind?* — and asks Grale nothing.** The reason is on the page: a man who has kept a condition for twenty-three years is not a man you walk up to in a copy room and ask a question. **The two words are a question, not a document, and there is still no box anywhere in the city for a question, and it is not a second pencil sentence that becomes a form.**

### Grale's referral, three of the four dates, and one of his own (Ch 92) — locked

- **Sarn's minute of the fourteenth of the second month is the answer and it came up the hill in a Crown March envelope with no name on the outside.** It records three finished dates and a half-written fourth: a day, a month, and then the word *the* and nothing, with the minute-taker's own note that the witness stopped him there and that he read the unfinished line back and the witness said it was correct. **That is *three and a half*, and it is the first time the phrase has been on the page.** The officer had read the list before he discussed it, and that is stated in the chapter and is the only thing anybody in this basin has ever been willing to say out loud about that office.
- **What goes at the head of a page (Ch 92, and it is the chapter's engine):** *the day, then the ruling, then the kind. You do not put a party at the top of a page because a page is not a claim, a page is a page. The day is the only thing on it that finds it again.* **Ostrowsky has never in nineteen years asked where a fourth clause went and asks a question in this chapter for the first time, and Grale asks him one.**
- **The consequence, and it is not the proof:** the three dates a man gave in a cell are **not dates of entries — they are the days at the heads of three pages, in Grale's own hand, in the office's practice — and not one of the eleven pages in the fifth box older than the eight-year practice has a date of its own.** The fourth page was begun by the officer of this office and the day on it is his, and it is the only one of the four nobody has ever asked about.
- **He does not finish it, and the reason is stated as a cost and not as a virtue:** the day he proves the line he has to stop, and stopping on the fourth would have left the ninth month's demand unanswerable. **He knows before he goes home that he has found a rule that lets him stay and call it duty, and he says it in the first person to nobody.** The letter he sends Crown March is four sentences, is the truest thing he has ever written, **and the fourth sentence is *the fourth page was begun by the officer of this office and I will not certify what is on it* — the first thing in nineteen years he has put about his own hand in an official record, and it cannot be taken out now that it has gone.** It is addressed to Ivo Sarn, who is in the coastal returns and is not the officer who wrote the minute.
- **The referral and the frame are in the same book and are not the same thing, and Grale lets them be in his head at the same time on purpose, once, and gets nothing for it.** Hesper's fourth date is not obtained and is not obtainable from that office.

### The chain arrives and the yard is charged twice (Ch 93) — locked

- **The lamp goes out on the sixth of the ninth month, eleven minutes after Grale arrives, and the office supplies a replacement in the third month and not before.** Grale walks up on foot because the rule is that the officer goes, and because a business has nobody to write beside its own line, **and he copies the order's wording onto a sheet in his own hand, which is not a copy of the order, because there is no copy and he has never made one and would not know how.**
- **What Fisk says, once, and asks never to have repeated:** it is not the field, the second sowing is in the ground and the ground does not want a lamp — **it is the bench.** A lot that does not come up goes through the Share's own rule into eleven days, a third of another member's kept-back, and a rate on a household's ground, **and there is no form in this kingdom in which a rate on a member's ground goes.** In about two years a woman on that road will be standing where Grale is standing with a figure she cannot pay in money and in seed she does not have, and nobody will be able to say who decided and when. *I am not asking you for anything. There is nothing in this room for a man from an office to do and you have come up a road for it and I would not have come down.*
- **The ninth link arrives, and this is the chapter nobody in Morrowfen knew: forty-three and some months, at a discount, with a house at the end of it that nobody in Tallow Ward has ever heard of, on a plate on the third shed of the carriers' row between the Gate and the north road. Bartel Reisz is fifty-eight, has thirty-one years in that shed, has been in arrears two seasons in thirty-one years and never three, and is a ratepayer and may read his own rate.**
- **And he cannot pay it, and the finding is his and it is exact:** on that plate what is owed is to the yard, so paying it stops the plate owing and pays the house nothing, and next season it is on there again. *You cannot pay your own debt. You can only pay somebody else's. And this is my plate, and it is on the ward's roll because a shed is not a household, and a shed cannot be asked, and that is not an injustice anybody did to me, that is just what a shed is.* **His written offer to pay it is in a book with no letter beside his name, no form exists on which he may be given it, and Marek — who knows it is in that book — cannot produce it either, and the book is Kelb's and Kelb is not in this chapter and is not asked.**
- **Kern puts the two charges in the open from the step in front of about ninety people, refuses to work out which the ward can afford — *because that is how you get a yard to say yes with its mouth while its hands are doing something else* — and says there is a third thing on a sheet on a hill that is bigger than both, that he does not know what it is, that he has asked nobody, and that he would like it minuted that he asked nobody.** The yard's refusal from the seed month is already on a nail and binds the yard and has never bound a household.

### The board cannot choose, and the Chair writes in longhand in a yard (Ch 94) — locked

- **A notice reaches four hundred and six people as a wet sheet weighted with a stone at a tank, because nobody can serve a notice on a yard, and it goes on above three boards that cannot be carried into a room.** A grandmother puts a cup on its corner and nobody moves the cup for two days. **About four hundred people are in a corridor and a stair outside a room built for thirty, and about ten are in the room, and nobody in the corridor can hear anything, and the door was shut, and that is a fact about the day and not a decision anybody took.** **Ilya Senn put the rope on the stair himself at the fifth bell the night before and said two sentences about it, one of which about two hundred people took as an insult.**
- **Kade's sheet, and it is the only document in the room with all four figures on it: forty thousand nine hundred · forty-three and some months · one hundred and forty a season · four hundred and six. No total at the foot and no sign between the first and the second.** She says it is a record and not an argument, and that if she had held a view the view would have an examiner's name over it, **and it is on the page that this is the fourth time a paragraph in a minute of that board has come from a seat that is not a membership.**
- **Why the table cannot choose is not a disagreement: the two charges are not the same kind of thing.** *One of them is a number and one of them is a sum. One has been standing for three hundred years and there is no party to it at either end, and if you apply it, it comes out of the account. The other has a party at both ends and a discount in the middle, and if you apply that, the house gets the money and the shed stops owing it.* **They are two different instruments put in the same place because there is one place for a rate to go in this basin and no others.** Sedge finds it in four minutes.
- **Kade's one sentence about money, from a seat that is not a membership, and it is right:** the room is being asked for a *comparison*; **a declining, in nine months, is going to be read as a refusal to decide, and it is not, it is a finding that two things that are not comparable have been put in front of a body with one seat standing empty.**
- **Term four does the work, in about eleven minutes.** Kern's grounds are five sentences in the first person and **not one word in them had been written down before**; Harrow reads them back twice, he corrects one word, and the Chair writes them in full with the duty to write them being the Chair's and not the member's. **The Chair then takes the paragraph out of the room and writes it again at the Gate tank in the rain, in longhand, on the back of a complaint sheet off a trestle, with a carpenter's pencil, with a hundred and forty people in the yard, and Kern stands beside him the whole time and does not read it upside down and that is not in any record.** It is read out twice and goes on the nail under the refusal and beside the sentence from the nineteenth of the harvest month, with a bit of string. **A Crown March representative wrote it and that is the only thing about it anybody in this basin can check.**
- **Marek is asked and says one sentence and it is the volume's:** *there is no office in this city that can produce the steps between that sum and this account, and that is not a mystery and it is not anybody's fault, it is a form* — followed by the yard's reason for refusing to be a schedule, and by a clerk on a hill who said he was not sure of the form and whose form was used anyway. **Harrow writes it into the minute in his own hand and there is no name at the foot of the paragraph and he does not say why and nobody asks.** The Trust's seat is minuted empty for the fifth time; neither charge paid, neither refused.

### The register goes live and the licence goes (Ch 95) — locked

- **The register is declared live for the eighteenth of the ninth month, by the Share and the yard, at the Gate tank, in about twenty minutes.** The Share carries the money, the yard gives the shed, **and a date is a day somebody else picked, and Bessok Tan wants that in the bench book in that order.** The hours are four a day, nothing is charged, and the fifth line of the form is empty because the hours are nobody's paper, and the levy is capped and the cap is the correct cap.
- **The yard says the hole out loud on the morning it goes live, from the step:** a document that cannot be produced and cannot be entered, and a hole in a live thing that is not an outrage but is a hole, and **in about six months a record of what this yard did in the ninth month will say that Tallow Ward's own method is not in the register, and that will be true, and the hole is the exact shape of the best paper the yard has produced.** The third condition is read out again unchanged.
- **WHO WITHDRAWS AND HOW, and the mechanism is the required reveal — why a licence can be withdrawn without a conviction:** the Chamber keeps a board by the posting desk of houses licensed to take up an assignment of a rate arrears and sell the resulting claim; **a licence is a permission and not a judgement, and compliance is a fact about an instrument and not a decision about a person, and if the desk is handed an instrument that complies the house is put back on the board in about a minute and nobody in the building can stop it because the clerk at the desk has no standing to stop anybody.** The third condition, printed on the licence and read aloud in the shed's own words: *that the claim be sold with its counterweight named upon the instrument sold, and the number of persons who will bear it stated upon the same.* The instrument before the desk is nine sheets and a schedule of fifty-two lines with a rate, a discount, a receiving party and **no counterweight at all.**
- **Nothing has been found against anybody and it is on the page in the finding:** no fraud, no misstatement, no figure that does not balance, a man in Bell Stair who counts a house's own figures twice with his own left hand and has said in a shed in Tallow Ward that this stack is the cleanest thing he has counted this year; the house is not charged with anything and there is no finding to appeal.
- **Ferrow is right and is not a defence:** a bundle cannot be made to comply in a week, because the answer is in it and the answer is a district, and a district is not a person; and a licensed house that will not sell a bundle can still sell a part, a part is a smaller thing with a smaller margin, **and this basin is about to have four prices for the same kind of thing instead of one and four prices are worse for the people at the bottom of them than one price was.** She has been wrong about this for nine years and says she is not going to be wrong about it gracefully.
- **The counterseal appears once and is not grown into:** the instrument carries a mark in a calendar nobody on Bell Stair can read, **and nobody at the desk says one word about it, because an audit in this city goes as far as the seal and stops.** The other two instruments with the hand on them are not in this chapter and are not mentioned. **Merrow does not mention the oldest page, is not told the page has been copied, and does not learn what the marks are.**
- **Merrow at the Gate shed is refused his own paper on the register's own first rule, in about ninety seconds, and says the two things that end the volume's argument with him:** *the price of an explanation in this city is going to be nothing and there is no form on which anybody may be charged for asking, and the only thing I have lost this morning is that*; and **he is not going to count the people in his own book now, and the reason is that there is nothing left to sell them into, and if he is honest with himself in a shed in Tallow Ward that is the entire reason.** He also says, and this is the volume's thesis in a lender's mouth: **the condition has required him to state the number of persons for nine years, he did not count them, and counting them is the work and the work is the part that does not get to be interesting.**
- **The entry on the board is four lines** and the fourth is *that the person who said it said it in the first person and meant it at the time and did not know whether he would still say it on the way home.* **The reader cannot still say whether he is a good man or only a competently defeated one, and that is the point:** Ferrow says *no* to his honesty in two words and will not give a reason, and he does not ask, and they go up the lane together having failed to say one true thing about each other. **He is not convicted, the Compact is not dissolved, he keeps the money, he is not a martyr, and he built the first register.**
- **He is entitled to be annoyed and says so in a shed in front of about eleven people.** *The register that is going to beat me is a yard's method with a name on it that is a tank and a date, and I could not have bought it off a bench in front of me, and I have said so in a shed in this month to a boy and I was not wrong.*

### Grale proves the line and stops, and the account is held by nobody (Ch 96) — locked

- **The line, stated once, in about four hours, at about the second bell on the twelfth:** every one of the eleven older pages in the fifth box has a day at the head of it in the office's hand and **not one has a date of its own**, so not one has a day on which it could be applied; the receiving party at the end of them is not a party but a word, a kind, the kind of rate that offsets a reserve and is recited on an assessment and is never itself charged to anybody; **the account says there is a sum against a district that nobody can apply and the box says the sum was handed over and came off the page, and both books are correct, and they have been saying opposite things about the same figure for three hundred years because one of them is a book entry and the other is a book entry and there is no fourth box on the form and there never has been.** The fourth of the four pages was begun by Grale and the day on it is his.
- **Grale's cut-order suspicion is NOT resolved and is now unprovable, and this is the point and it is on the page in the first person:** *I have believed for twenty-three years that the order in a frame gets cheaper as it goes down and that somebody worked that out about sixty years ago and arranged the order of the lines, and the school is at the bottom of it because of that. I have just proved the line I told a yard about in the rains and the line I have proved is a fifth box, and the line and the frame are in the same book. They are not the same thing.* **He says it out loud to one person within ten minutes of finding it, because if he does not, he will spend a month arranging to have said it — and a man who goes looking for his own condition is a man who has already decided the answer, and that is the only thing he has ever taught anybody in that building in nineteen years and he has never said it out loud before.** **The thing about the order of the lines is not going to be proved by him now and is not going to be proved by anybody after him, and he says that is the price of a condition he set himself in a yard in front of about forty people and would set again.**
- **His notice is dated the twelfth and takes effect on the day, and the reason is in the first person: he established a fact about this office's own practice and is no longer able to certify in the terms of the office.** No allegation is made against any person and there is nothing for anybody to investigate. The finding is in the margin of the district account in his own hand, because there is no form for a sentence and no form for a finding by a levy office officer.
- **He refuses to let Marek put the finding in the register, and the reason is the register's own rule turned against the register, in about eighty words:** a levy officer of nineteen years putting a sentence about a kind into a yard's room in the first person makes a thing that costs four hundred and six people money into a thing a yard's room said, **and a yard's room is a thing a man in a good coat can produce at anybody, and in nine months there will be a man in a good coat up that road who can say the ward's own method says a kind of rate is proper.** **Marek's answer is the fourth and last of their professional exchanges and it is not warm:** *that is the correct answer and it is the fourth time this year somebody has told me that to my face and every one of the four of them was somebody who was not going to have to carry it.* Grale's answer is *take it up with the yard* and then the paragraph about the demand, the plate and the second time in his life he did a correct thing that cost somebody else.
- **What happens to a district account when the only office that could hold it stops — the required reveal, and it is a small institutional fact and not a scene:** a district account is held by **a person**, not by a book, a room, a wall or a shelf, because the holder can be asked a question about it, can be asked to produce it, can be asked to enter a thing against it, and can be asked to show the steps between a sum in Rainmarket and a line in Bell Stair. **There is no form in this kingdom for a district account without an officer. A building is not a holder. A page is not a holder. A shelf is not a holder.** The water office writes on the fifteenth, and again on the seventeenth, **to the address of an office and not of a person**, and again in the tenth month, and **being annoyed requires somebody to be annoyed and there was nobody there to be annoyed.**
- **The recited carry stays unapplied, forty-three and some months stays on the plate, and a sum cannot be taken off an account except by a cut, and a cut carries the name of the officer who enters it.** The frame on the wall has one name under its last line and that name is four days old.

### Kade's own paper, in her own hand, under her own name (Ch 97) — locked

- **She goes up the upland road on the twelfth with an empty satchel, because a season examiner may not carry a lot she has examined and the paper is in her own district.** Hartlow, four terraces up, four miles from a stone bridge. **The room off the seed room, and the notice nailed to the outside of the door, has been up fourteen months: *ANYBODY MAY COME IN AND SIT AND LOOK. NOTHING IS CHARGED. NOTHING IS SIGNED.*** It came from a girl of fourteen who found out at twenty-five that she had been on a roll her whole life and had never been shown the paper, asked to see it, was refused, said *it was my life*, and was answered *then whose thing is it for*. **It has been read by four people in this basin. The Gate shed's notice has been read about nine hundred times. They are the same three rules, and one was nailed on with four nails by a man with a hammer and the other was cut in a copy room by a man who says on the page that a form has to be cut by a person who does not know whether it is right, and Kade reads both and does not say anything about the difference and nobody asks her to.**
- **Ruta Vrabec, fifty-one, on the roll of the Hartlow cooperative, a roll of six women, four over fifty and one a child, and the sixth line has an examiner's name on it and the examiner is not a member.** She filled cones for nineteen years and stopped at thirty-two and has given no reason anybody accepts. **She is the counterweight. The clause is *and the seed-keepers of this village after us bear the keeping*, and it is her and the boy, and the number is two, and one of them is nineteen and did not choose it and the other is fifty-one and says so in a shed in a ward she has never been inside.**
- **The room works out in about a minute who may enter the line, out of Marta Brel's Ch 87 rule, and Marta Brel is not there and is not needed:** the keeper cannot, because the counterweight is not the keeper — *the keeper is the man who is left holding the drawers* — and a person may enter a line about their own paper. **Kade is named at the door as a second person, does not get a hand on anything, and the fourth line is the only line the form gives her, and that is the whole of the mechanism and it is a punishment she chose and asked for.**
- **Her fourth-line sentence, in her own hand, under her own name, with the day, and nobody was asked for either and nobody could have been.** It is a correction published beside the entry, and **she asks for one thing and it is not forgiveness and it is not for her: that it go on the same sheet, in the open, with her name at the foot, because a correction on a paper of its own is a thing that can be lost, and because in a year somebody is going to read both lines and will have to read them in the wrong order to make her look better than she is.** It says the pledge was accepted as a class and not as an individual; that a class is not a person and she has known that for two years and it is the reason she wrote the sentence; **that the sentence is a confession and it is the only honest thing on the paper**; that she certified at the second bell two years back in that room before the room was open because there was no room; **that the witnesses were real and were not paid by the cooperative and she checked them twice, one is dead and the other is eighty-one and does not live there any more and she has not written to him in a year and a half**; that the paper is well made, the mechanism is lawful, every figure balances and nobody is to be punished; **that she saw what it was going to do, at the time, and wrote a sentence about it and went on certifying**; that she has had two years to write it in a room and did not; and that she is not asking anybody for anything.
- **He reads her entry out once and stops, and she asks him to read it again at the ordinary speed and not slower and not to make a moment of it, and he does.** *It is smaller than the mill and it is the smallest thing either of them has ever asked for and neither of them will refer to it again.* **No first names, no physical affection, no warmth, nobody thanked anybody, and the two of them do not look at each other.** This is the fourth and last time they are in a scene in this volume, after Ch 84, Ch 86 and Ch 88.
- **The sixth line, and it is the part she is angry about, once:** the days a person loses by standing in a room are ruled and empty, and this morning three people lost a day and a half of walking between them, **and there is no box in the kingdom for the days, and a man in a copy room cut that line in nine seconds and said he was not sure of the form, and he was right, and every person in the room finds it inside a week.**
- **Janko Vrabec, fifty-eight, the last of the four terraces, told in the growth month that he is going to die this winter, closes it by refusing to let it become the story of a woman of fifty-one who came down a hill**, and names the rope-maker of Ropewalk, the woman at the counter who has never been thanked, and the girl of sixteen nobody has ever asked, **and refuses to do the arithmetic out loud because there is a form in that shed with a rule about totals and the rule is right.**

### The second cut with no officer, and the school named for the second time (Ch 98) — locked

- **Ch 67's divergence is now PAID, and the reading is the one the state files recorded: Ch 67 is the first cut of MR 413 and Grale's second signed order in his life, and the second cut of the year is this one, and it has no officer on it.** Ch 67 also said that the hand went bad on the last letter and nobody saw it, and that the batch let the reader feel it was the last cut order that would have an officer to sign it without saying so. **It is now said: there is no third and there is no fourth, not because anybody has forbidden one and not because the water office has withdrawn it and not because a board of five has found anything, but because the name at the foot of the frame is the thing that makes the order an order.**
- **The second cut was drafted on the fifteenth of the ninth month by a man who is not an officer, in the words of the office, on a form that has not changed since before the flood years, in about an hour, and it is the same wording as the order in the frame because there is one form for a cut order and the office is not permitted a second.** The four things a cut order says are written out without being asked. **Ostrowsky asks where a fourth clause goes, at the first bell, for the first time in nineteen years, and the answer is the fourth of the four things, and the reason nobody is left in the building to ask is stated in the chapter.**
- **The office's list has fourteen lines and the school is the fourteenth and the last.** Read to seven parties: the low ground's night lamp (second from the bottom), the clinic's coal, the river gauge at the second junction, the four weighbridge lamps, the pumps at the sluice — **where the pumps man says *so the sluice is above the gauge* and is told *so the sluice is above the gauge*, again, and writes his hand beside the line, and nobody in this batch remarks on the echo and the reader may** — and the Elmsworth seed house's lamp, which is read again because the rule is that it is read and not because anybody has to be told twice.
- **Pella Rusk, in a corridor at the sixth bell, in terms:** they have named the school twice, once in a finding in a room on a hill with a Chair writing it in his own hand and once on the last line of an order in a frame in a building she has never been inside, **and neither time was anybody asking her anything, and both times the school was the bottom of the list**; a school is not a party, so she cannot decline, and term three of that board lets a party decline and the declining gets written down as a declining, **and there is no form in this kingdom in which a school declines**; the only way she can say no to a thing that is not a party is by saying it to a man's face, **and a man's face is not a record and it is gone at the seventh bell**; and in a year somebody will open a book and find the school was named as a party and was never asked, and she wants that on the sheet in her own words. **The ninety-one and the fourteen are said for the third time and nobody asked for them.** Her declining is written in her own words because the office is required to write down what she said.
- **Marek is at the other end of the corridor, because a man with no standing in a building is the sort of person who stands at the other end of a corridor. She does not ask him about page three, and he notices that it is a decision and not a courtesy, and works out in about four seconds that she has traded the answer she is owed for a number in a sheet and that the trade was hers.** He offers her the register and tells her, before she asks, what it cannot do: the order is not the school's paper, it is a district's account, a district is not the school, the school is not a person, a line about it would have to be entered by the ward, and a ward is not a person either, **and the yard has already found that out in ninety seconds in front of about eighty people and it is on a nail.** She says *thank you*, which is correct and which neither of them wanted to hear. **She goes back in and watches the clerk walk away and he walks the other way, and the answer she was owed in a schoolroom in the rains month was not given, and this time she had not asked for it, and that was the whole of the difference and it was hers. Marek's unanswered answer to Pella Rusk is STILL UNANSWERED and is not paid off, and this is the last time it is touched in this volume.**
- **The order is not entered, and the chapter says why in four lines:** a cut is not a date; nothing falls on a day; nothing falls at the close of a month, and that is not the reason; **the lines fall when somebody sends somebody, and this year nobody has sent anybody, and there is no form on which anybody may be applied for.** The frame goes back on the wall over the pale rectangle with one name under its last line, four days old. **The fourteenth line of the list is a school off the north lane with about ninety-one children on its roll and about fourteen places, and a cut that has not come off the ground is not a threat, it is a thing that has not happened yet.**

### The wrong date, the crew, and three seasons of eleven days (Ch 99) — locked

- **Why the date is wrong, and it is a custom and not a fraud:** supervised road work on a Crown March road is Crown March money; an office in Crown March releases the money and countersigns the document it releases it against, **and it dates the day the money went out, the sixteenth, and not the day the board of five in Bell Stair found the schedule lawful, which was the seventeenth; that office has not heard of that board and there is no form on which a room in Bell Stair advises an office in Crown March that a schedule under its consideration has been found lawful, and there is no box for it in either place.** Both dates are true. The paper carries one of them, on the front.
- **He refuses three remedies in order and gives a reason for each:** a letter, because then a man in a town he has never been to has changed a date because a bridgewright asked nicely in a hand he can read; an inquiry, because he is a bridgewright and the answer will be no and the no will be correct and the no will be in a record for ever with his name at the bottom of it asking whether Crown March money is dated properly; **and a clerk, in a record, saying so, which is the only thing he wants and which he has been told does not exist.** He will be a man about it in a way the crew has not seen, and he tells them beforehand that he knows it.
- **The work is untouched and the losses are not fixed by a date:** three seasons of eleven days, sixty-six days, six men named, residual on the Gate board, the money out on the sixteenth and the work started on the twentieth, and no form in this kingdom that can un-lay stone. **Twenty-six months of his own recovery posted on the fourteenth and are not cancelled and do not come back to anybody. Bohum Ostrek, six years on the road, signed at a counter-desk in the rains and lost two weeks of the middle of his hand, lawfully, under a clause nobody has ever questioned, and the clerk who countersigned it was a boy of twenty who did it correctly and is at his desk in Bell Stair this morning.**
- **Lio, nine, with a slate, is the chapter's question and she is not asked to be anywhere:** *what is a date for.* *It is for afterwards, when somebody wants to say the work on this road was not allowed to be done.* *Afterwards is us.* *Yes.* And she writes on the slate, in a child's hand, ***afterwards is us, so tell them the true day.*** Nobody says anything to her about it and nobody stops her.
- **The only correction available, and it is Harrow's and it is in a minute:** a Crown March representative who has to record in his own minute that a Crown March date and a finding of his own board may be read together, and that they bear dates one day apart, and that he has had the document about an hour and has established nothing with it and does not know whether the office knows a board sat, **is a representative who has said that a thing in his own region is capable of being read two ways.** He records the discrepancy **at the request of a party to the work and not at the request of any office**, states that he has made no enquiry and has no authority to make one and is not going to have one, and says he will not put a bridgewright's question into a Crown March file. **It is the last of five paragraphs and has no name at the foot of it, and the paragraph before it has no name at the foot of it either, and the one before that has a Fund secretary's name under it.** Nobody countersigned it, because no office will serve the record of a body it is a party to. It went into a drawer at the seventh bell on the seventeenth. **Marek was not in the room; he delivered the document in the inside pocket of a coat with four sentences and left, and the fourth sentence was that he was a party to the work and not an office and would be thanked for nothing. He is told at a tank at the seventh bell by a rope-maker, and says *that is very good of him*, and Sanne Droll says *do not*, and the man at the counter has never worked out which of the two was the kindness.** Nobody thanked anybody and nobody has referred to it again.

### The first morning, and nothing at the head of it (Ch 100) — locked

- **The register's first morning as a live register is the eighteenth of the ninth month, and it is not the fourth morning of looking (Ch 90) and the two are never confused in the prose or in a state file.** The live date was set on the ninth (Ch 95). About eighty in the lane at the fourth bell for a shed that holds twenty-two. A second notice went up on the sixteenth in a different hand saying *the eighteenth*, and got the date right by being wrong, and nobody has taken it down.
- **Forty-one lines in forty-one hands is the count on the fourth morning of looking in the harvest month and it was the last number that room had; the register carried forty-two in forty-two hands in Ch 97 on the twelfth of the ninth month, and on its first live morning there were a great many more than that and no figure at all, and the register's live count is not given and is never going to be**, and the reason is the register's own and it is said out loud to the lane about eleven times in four hours: *there is a line in here in a hand for every person who has come through that door, and a bundle is not one line, and a hand is the only kind of entry there is.* **The register keeps no total and no figure about the register is ever to be stated.**
- **Wenna Drel, sixteen, comes at the fifth bell with a slate with her name on it and puts it on the trestle in her own hand, and Bessok Tan puts the roll down flat, which is the second time in a season he has done that in front of a person, and will not write her on it, and tells her that nobody is going to ask her and the yard is not going to and he is not going to, and that if anybody ever does it will be a different thing and she can say no to it.** She takes the slate back and stands four feet off the counter in a room of strangers for a quarter of an hour. **Nobody follows her, nobody writes anything down, the cap is still the correct cap, the person it does not reach is still standing at a trestle, and the slate is still on the trestle and Bessok Tan has not put it in the box and has not thrown it away and has it still. Nobody may resolve this by asking her and nobody did.**
- **The blank column, and it is the volume's conclusion in one object:** a boy of fifteen from Ropewalk who is not a clerk ruled a column on the wall list in the second week for the kind of paper each line is about, **and there is nothing at the head of it.** Somebody proposes *rate* and then *loan* in the ordinary way, with a reason, and the room goes quiet, because **a heading over a column of people's papers is a line, and it is the one kind of line that cannot be entered at all, because there is no person behind it.** **Nineteen people decline to name it over about half an hour, nobody counts it out loud, nothing is written down, and one of the nineteen is the boy who ruled it, who said *it is not mine to name*.** **The yard then votes in four minutes not to fill it AND not to rule it out**, and the second vote is the one that matters, **because a promise not to name one is a heading too and a room can be held to it in a year by somebody who was not there on the day.** This is the yard's Ch 35 vote of the rains month on a different board and the yard says so and will not let anybody tell it it is a different principle.
- **Four bare places in one basin in one year, and not one of them has ever been given a heading. The list is Chapter 100's, in Chapter 100's order, and it is the list to inherit:** a sheet cut off a seed sack in a keeper's house in the growth month; **a column on a board in a shed in Tallow Ward in the seed month**; this column; and a column on a bench in a shed on the low-ground road, bare at the head of it since the day two painters were sent home — **which is Merrow's shed and the column Movement IV has called the seventh since the twentieth of the seed month, and Chapter 100 does not name it and is describing the same place.** **The register's fifth line for the hours is NOT one of the four**: the hours are at the head of it, so it is a named line, and it is empty because the hours are nobody's paper, which is a different fact and not the same thing. **An earlier version of this line listed the fifth line where the Tallow Ward board belongs and dropped the board's seed-month date, and the sentence that went with it claimed none of the four had ever been named, which is false, because the fifth line is named and was never on the list; the state files and the chapter disagreed and Volume 03 would have inherited whichever list it read. Only the Tallow Ward board is named here and not in any earlier chapter, so a later batch may carry the fact that the place is bare and unnamed and may not build on it. Nothing in this batch proposes a heading for any of the four and no character may.**
- **The refusal is still on the nail, and there is now a space on the wall inside the shed where it would go, and the space is under a column with nothing at the head of it, so it cannot go in there either, and the yard did not know that until the eighteenth of the ninth month.** The register is to live with a hole in it and the hole is the shape of the yard's own best paper. **It is still not entered, in any form, and no office holds it, and this is the volume's conclusion and Movement V did not fix it.**
- **What the room has cost, named out loud from the step by Kern on a live morning and not improved:** about nine people stopped coming and nobody knows which nine, **one of the two people who carry the yard's water going up the lane with two pailfuls past a queue of about eighty, and nobody in the queue asked anybody to move**; about nine more who were in the lane at the fourth bell and not at the seventh, and the room has still not found a way to know which nine; the fifth line empty because the hours are nobody's paper; the sixth line empty for ever; and a day and a half of walking with nowhere to put it. **And none of this makes a person safe to lend to, and that is said at the tank on a live morning and is the sentence the whole volume ends on.**
- **The last image, and it is a man signing.** A man of about forty from the Tanner's yard, who is a household and is known, and who had it at the fourth bell and did not at the fifth, comes down the lane with a rate arrears on a plate on a shed door. **A person in the lane has a table and a bundle of Compact forms under it, and a form is a sheet of paper and a sheet of paper is not a house, and the Morrowfen assignment licence has lapsed, and there is no form in this kingdom on which a person may be stopped.** Merrow comes out in a good coat and says four things in about fifteen seconds: **the form is not his house and there is no form on which a person may be stopped; the paper is a rate arrears on a shed and the return box is the correct box and it is correct this morning and nobody in this city can say one word against it including him, and he cannot explain it for free because the man has not come to a room and asked; he is not going to witness it, and that is not a kindness but a rule and an old one, and it has cost him money before he has sold anything; and he is not going to stop him and could not if he tried, because a man in a lane may sign anything and there is nobody in this basin with the standing to say he may not, and the only three things that could have been asked are a ward, a shed and a district, and none of the three is a person.** **The man signs. Merrow goes back inside and has said since neither that it was right nor that it was wrong.** About nine people in the lane saw it and about twenty-one were in the shed being read to and did not, **and the reader heard it and read the fourth line again, twice, at the ordinary speed.**
- **Marek is at the counter, about nine feet off, with about nine seconds in which he could have walked out and said one sentence, and he does not go out and he writes nothing down, and the chapter gives the whole reason and it is the volume's last institutional cruelty: a line may be entered by the person behind it and about their own paper; the man is the person behind it and he is not in the room and is not going to be; there is no form on which anybody may be stopped; and the register's one rule — a bundle may not be entered as one line, the counterweight named to the people who will bear it — has nothing whatever to say about a shed's rate arrears, which is where the Compact's money came from in the first place.** **A register is not a seed, and the volume ends on a queue and on a piece of paper with a fresh signature on it and on a column with nothing at the head of it.**
- **The coda, in the order the volume leaves things:** the recited carry still unapplied; forty-three and some months still on a plate at a discount with a house at the end of it; **a district account held by nobody**; a frame on a wall in Bell Stair with one name under its last line; a lamp out in Rainmarket until the third month; a woman of thirty-four working sixty-one trays twice a day in a cold shed in the dark; a boy of nineteen reading out a fourth line twice at the ordinary speed; a man of fifty-eight at home who has stopped.

### What every character ends the volume with

- **Marek Vale: a clerk with no office, no post, no pay, no form, no work and no room.** He has, by other people and by no form, the standing duty of a man who reads the register out, given at a tank in about a hundred and forty people's hearing and refused to be put in ink (Ch 82) and recorded as the last of five paragraphs of a minute with no name at the foot of it (Ch 88). **In Movement V he reads a woman's admission out loud and asks for it to be read twice at the ordinary speed; he writes two words on the back of a list that are a question and not a document; he asks a man of fifty-eight one question and is told the correct answer for the fourth time this year by somebody who was not going to have to carry it; he is told at a tank at the seventh bell that a Crown March representative did something and is told by a rope-maker not to say thank you; he delivers a countersignature up a hill in his coat with four sentences and asks for nothing; and at about the sixth bell on the first morning of a live register he stands nine feet off a counter and watches a hungry man sign a paper, and does not go out, and writes nothing down.** **He gets no office, no post, no pay, no vote, no right of entry, no way of making the ward's own answers producible, and no way of turning a market's consequence into a smaller thing. The card was spent in Ch 89 and brought him nothing and is not mentioned in Movement V.**
- **Aldis Kern and the ward: charged twice, refusing to choose in front of about a hundred and forty people, again, and in longhand, in the yard's own words.** The yard's refusal is on a nail with a bit of string, the Chair's hand is on the nail under it, the sentence from the nineteenth of the harvest month is beside it, and there is a space on the register's wall where the refusal would go and it cannot. **The yard is not grateful, not thanked, not forgiven and not a party, and it does not intend to be. The independent-witness box is still empty on the worse reason. Sanne Droll is still the second person at the register and was not re-asked in Movement V and was not thanked. The register is a yard's method with a name on it that is a tank and a date and not a person, and the yard has now voted twice, in the rains month and in the ninth, against naming a heading, and it will not name the pen.**
- **Ovid Merrow: he loses a monopoly he is entitled to, keeps the money, and says the loss was fair.** He is not convicted, he is not refuted by a lie, he does not go in a good coat to anybody's rescue, and **the reader still cannot say whether he is a good man or only a competently defeated one, and that is the point.** His register is the best office in this basin for this work and he says so. **The price of an explanation in this basin goes to nothing in this volume and that is the whole of what he loses.** He is not written as a martyr. He is not a martyr, and he built the first one.
- **Oren Pike: he keeps his profit and is not forgiven and is not written as a good man, and he is not in Movement V.** His figures and his derivation stand and no later batch may re-derive the hundred and forty from four a week. **He is a factor and not a lender, and no second lender enters the volume.** The person who buys the shed's rate paper in Ch 100's lane is a person with a table and a bundle of forms, is not a house, is not a lender, and is not named.
- **Bel Harrow and the board: the same four terms, the Trust's seat empty for the fifth time, no fund and no date, and a board asked to choose between two charges it has no standing to compare and cannot pay.** One term does the work, and it did it twice: Kern's grounds in the finding, and a paragraph of a minute with no name at the foot of it. **The Chair serves every minute in his own hand, has no clerk, cannot get one, wrote a ward captain's objection on the back of a complaint sheet off a trestle in the rain, wrote a Crown March date into a Crown March record nobody asked for, and is thanked by nobody and has not mentioned either again.**
- **Neris Kade: Chapter 97 is hers.** She produced her own district's paper herself and went up the road herself and asked nobody to carry anything; she is named at the door as a second person and gets a hand on nothing; **she is not forgiven by anybody in the volume, including herself, and she asks for nothing and asks for one thing that is not for her, which is that her sentence be on the same sheet with her name at the foot of it.** She is an examiner, not a member, cannot vote, and nobody thanked her. **She is right about the register and was unable to do anything about it and she is right about the days and there is nowhere to put them.**
- **Ondra Kelb: not asked about the hold, not in Movement V, and his left hand is still a nine-year-old's and is not his.** He is referred to once, in Ch 93, as the clerk who wrote a man's offer to pay in a book with no letter beside his name, and the offer cannot be produced by anybody. **He is not a source, not a witness and not a party, and his fifth column with nothing at the head of it is not in Movement V and is not named.**
- **Grale: he has wanted to stop since he was thirty-five and Chapter 96 is the day.** His condition is honoured to the letter and it is his own doing. **The district account is then held by nobody, the water office's second cut of the year has no officer to sign it, and that is the volume's last institutional cost.** The carry's origin is frozen; the cut order getting cheaper as it goes down is **not** resolved and is now unprovable, and that is the batch's own cruelty and not a resolution.
- **Halvard Sunn: no obligation, and he is not in Movement V.** **The counterseal appears once, on the instrument at the posting desk, and nobody at the desk says a word about it because an audit in this city goes as far as the seal and stops.** The other two instruments in that hand are not in Movement V, nothing in Movement V may grow into the Volume 03 question, and **nobody in Morrowfen knows who A-0 is and the mark is not mentioned once in ten chapters.**

### Ledger Sight, Boundary Hold, and the guardrails across Chapters 91–100

**Zero Ledger Sight panels in ten chapters. The budget was at most one with zero as the target and it is met at zero. No Boundary Hold is used, wished for, hinted at or mentioned, and the founding standing hold is not referred to at all. Marek's forearm is not described, referred to or looked at anywhere in the ten chapters, and no second person decodes either entry in the vault book on the second floor. Tamsin is not mentioned and is asked nothing. The clerk of record is not advanced.** The cost of the power is paid entirely in hours and in being told the correct answer by somebody who does not have to carry it.

**The volume's guardrails are intact across the final ten chapters.** Nobody in Morrowfen knows who A-0 is. **Merrow learned nothing**: he does not mention the oldest page, is not told the page has been copied, and does not learn what the marks are. The ward roll naming district four and the name Kallow was not mentioned once. Pella Rusk's unsigned surety cell is untouched, is not a surprise, and her unanswered answer is not paid off. Zorka Vendl's discharge book, the Hollow Galleries, the unclaimed take-off, the duty clerk's unplaced roll, Nim Rook and Ilen Rook, and the mother's refusal about the mark are all untouched. **No Choir, no military body, no regional political crisis, no Crown of Continuation, no second lender and no Saltglass Harbor Council; a harbor notary is not the Council and a factor is not a lender and a person with a bundle of forms in a lane is neither.** **No office was closed, nobody was convicted, the Compact is not dissolved, and the register does not look like a fix.**

**There is no villain in this batch and none was manufactured.** The young man with the tube is a courier who does not understand and is not a party. Merrow is right and loses. Ferrow is right and is not a defence. Sedge is right and is not a hero. Pike is not in it. Grale is right and pays. Ostrowsky is right and asks a question nineteen years late. Kern, Droll, Fisk, Tan, Rusk, Lask, Brel, the Vrabecs, Ostrek, Reisz and Kade all act correctly and all cost the reader something. **The only criminal available is a rule that puts a cost in a box with no person in it, and this batch adds three: a heading at the top of a column; a district account without an officer; and a form in a lane, which is a sheet of paper and not a house, on which a man may sign anything and there is no form on which anybody may be stopped.**

### Deliberate divergences and notes for the reviewer

- **Length, counted from the files with the title line included: 29,874 words across ten chapters, and the batch is OVER the 24,000–28,000 plan by 1,874.** The distribution is **Ch 91 2,538 · Ch 92 2,430 · Ch 93 2,512 · Ch 94 3,195 · Ch 95 3,409 · Ch 96 3,171 · Ch 97 3,560 · Ch 98 2,401 · Ch 99 2,867 · Ch 100 3,791.** The shortest is 2,401 and the longest is 3,791; **no chapter is under 2,400, three are above 3,400 and five are above 3,100.** This is the batch's overrun and it is recorded rather than rounded down, and it is smaller than batch 0003's 30,279 and smaller than batch 0004's 30,510, and larger than batch 0002's 26,688 and batch 0001's 24,937, **all four counted with the title line included, which batch 0002's own note above is not and states its own method.** **The reason is not padding: Ch 95 carries a decision in a yard, a licence at a counter and a man in a shed, and the three are the volume's climax and cannot be cut apart; Ch 97 carries a road, a room, a counter and a correction and is the volume's character chapter; Ch 100 carries the first morning, a blank heading, a girl at a trestle and a signature in a lane, and it is the last chapter of a volume.** The files were trimmed during drafting and **these counts were taken from the files after the last edit and again after the batch 0005 review, which moved Ch 99 from 2,826 to 2,867 and the batch from 29,833 to 29,874; the previous version of this line gave a longest of 3,774 and two chapters above 3,400 and contradicted the list in the same sentence, and this file is where the rule against trusting a self-reported number in it was learned.**
- **Ten engines and no long table.** A levy office, a courier, and a word that has changed in a document; a tray, an envelope, a lamp and a night in an empty copy room with one colleague; a lane, a tank, a seed house and two people learning what is on a plate; a hearing room built for thirty with about four hundred in a corridor, a rope on a stair, and a Chair writing in longhand in the rain at a tank; a morning at a posting desk with a licence and a board; a desk at the second bell, a book, and a man writing a notice in his own hand at noon; a road with eleven switchbacks and a notice nailed to a door; an office wall, a frame, a fourteen-line list and a school corridor; a bridge span in the wind, a crew of six, a slate and a parcel carried up a hill; a lane at the fourth bell, a counter, a queue of eighty, a trestle and a form. **No chapter's load-bearing event is a document read out to a room, and no copy-room scene and no long table in the batch.** Ch 92 is two men and a book and it is a decision not to look. Ch 96 is a man finding something and a man writing a notice and it is the absence of a person from a room. Ch 98 is a frame and a wall and a corridor. Ch 99 is a span and a wind. **No chapter ends on a figure or on a notice spoken to a crowd**, and the last chapter ends on a piece of paper in a lane.
- **Panel budget: ZERO Ledger Sight panels in ten chapters.** No Boundary Hold. The founding standing hold is not used, offered, wished for or hinted at. Marek's forearm is not described, referred to or looked at. Tamsin is not mentioned and is asked nothing.
- **Kalendar: the batch's calendar check is clean for the first time in the volume.** A grep of Chapters 91–100 for any real-world month name or any weekday, excluding *Crown March* as a street and a district, returns **nothing**. **Two errors were written and fixed during drafting and the grep that caught them is recorded here because the previous three batches each shipped at least one: Ch 96's *on a Tuesday he is not going to describe to anybody* and Ch 93's *if I go in there on Monday*** — one a claim about a day, one a claim about a day. **There is no *May* false positive in this batch, because the title that produced it, *Who May Write In It*, is Chapter 84 and is not in this batch.** The ninth month is the volume's clock and no figure for the distance to it is given anywhere in the ten chapters.
- **Figures the batch creates and a later batch must not break:** **No new money figure is created in this batch.** The only sum named here that is not already in the manuscript is **forty-three and some months**, which is a carriers' row rate arrears out of Chapter 61 and appears in Chapters 93, 96, 98 and 100 unchanged; **the ninth month's letter, its two words, *advise* and *is to be charged*;** the register's live date of **the eighteenth of the ninth month**; **fourteen lines on the office's list, the school the fourteenth and the seed house's lamp the thirteenth;** **seven parties on the second cut;** **four figures on Kade's sheet, 40,900 · 43 and some months · 140 a season · 406;** **nineteen hands that declined to name the column and a yard vote in four minutes;** **eleven older pages in the fifth box, four of them the four Hesper named, and a half-written fourth date in a minute;** **an hour at a table with a Crown March document on it and no enquiry made;** **a day and a half of walking between three people;** **about eighty in the lane on the first morning for a shed that holds twenty-two.** **The locked figures of Movements I–IV are unchanged: 40,900, 2,400, 1,916, 300, 1,100, 40, 61, 52, 9, nine links, eleven of sixty-one trays, forty of forty-one, thirty-eight and fourteen out of fifty-two, two a reading, sixty to ninety a week, four a week, one hundred and forty a season, about a hundred and sixty a year, four shillings, about a hundred and forty people, and the forty figures that do not close. Nothing in this batch makes any of them close and no chapter in the batch chose one of the four figures that do not close.**
- **Two outline divergences to record rather than paper over, and a third that is a correction of the outline's own date.**
  1. `outline/volume-02.md`'s final chapter image places the register's first morning in the harvest month. **Chapter 100 is in the ninth month, which is the volume's clock and the climax's month, and the live date was set on the ninth of the ninth month in Ch 95 and the first live morning is the eighteenth.** Ch 90 is a fourth morning of looking and is not a first morning, and the two are never confused in the prose.
  2. `outline/volume-02.md` says the concrete resolution includes *Hesper Rann's four dates are closed at three and a half.* **Three and a half is on the page and it is the shape of Sarn's minute — three finished dates and a half-written fourth — and not a finding about Hesper, and the fourth date is still Hesper's to give or not to give and nobody has taken it off him.** Hesper is not in Movement V and is not asked.
  3. `outline/volume-02.md` says *Grale proves the line in the account desk.* **The line is not named in the outline and the chapter names it, and this is a canon addition and not a repair: the recited carry and the fifth box's standing entry are the same figure seen from two ends, both books are correct, and neither is a levy, a schedule, a posting or a return, and there is no fourth box on the form and there never has been.** The outline does not record it and `outline/volume-02.md` was not edited. **The carry's origin is still not explained and this is not an explanation of it; it is what an officer can see from inside the room in four hours, and the sixth and the frame are a different thing and he says so.**
- **The prompt's batch-specific requirements, checked one at a time on the page:** the ninth month is named (91); nobody may prove the line before 92 (he does not) and nobody may connect the referral to the cut order (92 and 98 are in different rooms and the connection is refused on the page at 96); nobody in Morrowfen finds out before 93 that Tallow Ward is the end of it (93); the Chair does not vote in a money matter and the Trust's seat is empty (94); Merrow is not convicted, the Compact is not dissolved, Ferrow does not approve, and he does not learn what the marks are (95); the district account is then held by nobody and the second cut has no officer (96, 98); the school is named as a party for the second time (98); the flat board is on the trestle under the form and nobody picked it up (90) and **it is not in Movement V and is not produced in any room**; the ninth link is collectable and has not been collected and is not collected in Movement V either — **Reisz's sum is still on the plate at the end of the volume and is still uncollected, because a sum cannot be taken off an account except by a cut and there is no officer, and that is the last institutional cost.**
- **A self-reported pass in this section is not evidence.** The findings above were produced by writing the ten chapters and grepping them, and the counts were taken from the files after the last edit. **A reviewer should re-read before treating any of it as closed** — and the batch 0004 review is the proof of that sentence rather than a contradiction of it, because it found ten blocking defects and seven smaller ones in a batch that had described itself as clean.
- **`state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and untouched. The batch summary lives in `state/current.md` because no separate batch-summary file exists in the repository layout.**
