No copy-room scene at a long table; no corridor in any of the ten chapters. No chapter opens with a date and a feeling. No chapter ends on a figure or on a notice spoken to a crowd; Ch 120 ends on the bench book and the fourth-bell opening with the fifth line empty.
- **Every verification sentence above was produced by grepping the files after the fix pass.** A reviewer should re-read before treating any of it as closed.

## VOLUME 03, BATCH 0003 (Chapters 121–130, Movement III — The Direction)

Prose is authoritative over the batch cards wherever they disagree. Chapters run from the sixth of the growth month, MR 414, to the eleventh of the harvest month. **No event is placed after the nineteenth of the growth month** (the growth month is twenty days long and its last ten are the eleventh to the twentieth; its length is not restated). **No other month's length is stated and no days are counted backwards across a month boundary.** The batch is the volume's power change and it is the first batch of the series in which Marek does anything at all to a person.

### Season Hand, as it is actually built on the page (Chs 121–126)

- **It is not granted, taught, named, or entered anywhere.** He has had it since the tenth month of MR 413 and spent four months believing it was Ledger Sight. What he has is a **sighting** of where an already-authorised effect is standing, and the finding in Ch 121 is that seeing is not knowing and **a sighting cannot be entered in a column in this kingdom.**
- **The effect.** The MR 412 upland instrument is unsettled — *settlement: out of the harvest*, and the harvest of the season it bought was the tenth month of MR 413 and this year's is not in. **A season is not a thing that sits in a room; a season is an effect somebody obtained in writing, on a form, in advance, for a scope, against a pledge, and it is still travelling.** He can point it. He cannot increase it, cannot double it, and cannot choose a destination for what is left.
- **The four words, and the discovery of them by failure (Ch 121, 123).** `A name, and then the season you have got.` **Not** `this season, this lot, here` — the failed form talks to an object that the effect is already standing in, and an effect does not answer to being told to arrive where it already is. The four words are the whole of it and he cannot put them in fewer, and the four days of not knowing whether he had done a thing are the worst four days of that year.
- **The cost, first time (Ch 123): a season and not a person.** It is **not a hole.** A hole is a payment and there is a name and a form and a length for one. What he lost is a season of *knowing a thing*: he cannot produce his own habits without first arriving at them. **It is a season of a man's hand, not of his life.** Nothing to show anybody. He writes four words in pencil under his own figures on the back of his left hand — *season, third bell, four days* — which is not the book and not a document and is for nobody.
- **The cost, second time (Ch 126): it attaches.** The second direction — aiming the season back into the low ground, where it came from — does not work four times over, and then the ditch goes out from under him: **the hoe, a gate latch, the weight of a bucket, the way a woman turns a sack off her hip, the sound a scythe makes going well. All gone, all not his, all shaped like a habit that was never his.** The season is a thing and a thing can only be in one place, and incompatible directions attach the debt to the holder. **The nine rows on the second terrace stay up. The basin gets nothing. He is out a season he did not get to give away.**
- **The third cost, and the only Boundary Hold in the volume (Ch 128).** **Two and a half days on a bank at the foot of the third terrace, continuously, from about the sixth bell on the second of the harvest month to about the fourth on the fifth, by a man who does not sleep, because a man who sleeps is a hand that is not there.** Somebody brought bread and a pot up on the second night; Tomas brought a coat on the fourth and neither of them said a word. **It is boring — no heat, no light, no sound — and the whole of what he gets to look at is nine men with scythes on a slope.** He chooses the length and never the destination; the water is not gone, it is held the way a door is held, and it comes back at the rate it had been coming and is four feet up the slope by about the ninth bell on the sixth, which is three days and four hours later than it would have been and is the same four feet. **Nobody is charged, and the reason is not an arrangement and not a kindness: the cost of a hold can never reach a person who did not borrow, guarantee, witness, or certify, and not one of the nine men with scythes did, and not one of the four people on the third terrace did, and no acre of that ground has ever borrowed anything off anybody — and he is the only party, and he made himself the only party on the ninth.** Released, he cannot for four seconds produce his brother's face.

### The three things the directions cost, and where they landed

- **A household gains a season it did not ask for (Ch 123–124).** **Stana Peric**, thirty-one, second terrace, on her mother's line, five children, eldest nine, nine of the thirty-nine lots that came up as sand. **She is NOT the holder of the eighth line. The eighth line is the second terrace's store and it has a name on it because a store room with a lock has a holder and a holder is a person (Ch 113), and the name on it is her mother's** — fifty-eight, and she does not count, which is a compliment, and it took Marek about a minute to work out that it is one. **Stana has no store of her own for exactly that reason (Ch 122), and Ch 124 says the finding out loud: he had put a season on a household in order to save a store that somebody was going to empty anyway.** The set-off notice has been on the seed house nail since the sixth of the third month and the store is not going to be emptied by it, and nobody in that family can say why, and she has decided not to think about it until the seed is in and shut. **CORRECTED at the third repair pass: this entry, `current.md`, `chapter-summaries.md` and `open-threads.md` all said Stana holds the store, against Ch 122, Ch 124 and Ch 113. The mother holds it. Do not reverse this.**
- **The counterweight does not shrink; it moves, and it moves onto a household that cannot see it coming (Ch 124).** **Mileva Vukic**, thirty-eight, **first terrace**: a spring that has come up in that field every year of her life comes up brackish; a forty-year-old pear comes out in leaf a second time; a boundary stone has moved nine inches in ground nobody touched; a heifer does not come back in heat. **None of it has happened yet** — a wall falls in a year and a man in a good coat can be shown it, and next year's ground cannot be entered, served, sworn to, or shown anywhere in this kingdom. **The jar on the rail is the loneliest object in the basin.** She walked four miles on the seventeenth to ask Zofia Teran to write the day down; Teran wrote four lines with nothing at the head of them and said out loud that the only answer anybody will ever get to *was the first terrace's water bad* is that a woman she has known nineteen years told her so, and **there is no form on which she can be served with the question.**
- **A district loses its season and nobody is told (Chs 124, 126, 130).** The low ground's second sowing did not come; about a fifth of the first did not; the third was thin. **The chaff stall on the low-ground road shut four days running and then it was not a stall any more.** Margit Sova, forty-one, wrote it on her list with nothing at the head of it and told nobody. **The carts turned round on the ninth of the harvest month, and Ottmar Kuez read the same rate to a cart going up with straw and a cart coming down with barley, and the rate did not move in either direction, and nine people in a lane did not go and look at the bridge book, which has never happened in nine years.**

### The register's rule, used by a person it was not written for (Ch 129)

- **The yard refuses Marek.** He asks to enter a line about his own paper and has neither a form nor a paper. *Suppose I write it on a sheet of my own.* Then the four questions at the counter, and the **fourth one is the one nobody has ever heard Sanne Droll ask and it is *is there a person behind it who is in this room*** — the answer is no, because the person behind it is four hundred and some people on four terraces and about forty people are in the shed and none of them are on four terraces. **It is the first line the yard has ever refused him, and the yard refused it and not anybody, and Droll says she is not going to say a word about why he did not put his name to it, because if she says why not she is a person who knows.** **The rule is not extended, is not enlarged by a man with no office, and is not turned into a remedy. It is applied to the man it was made for, and it holds, and he loses.**
- **And then the yard refuses Petar Duss, on the yard's own reason, for the opposite reason.** He is the party. He signed, he paid four shillings, he stood on a bridge. The refusal is the mark: **nine letters in a corner and a man in two rooms over a chandler's shop who has said out loud that he does not know what the marks are and has not known for nine years.** Droll states plainly that **this is a new call she has never had to make in thirty-nine years at that counter, that she does not know that she is right and has not checked, and that what she has checked is that the mark is the only part of the sheet with a person in it, and a form and a figure cannot be anybody's.** Duss asks for the refusal itself to be written down; the yard cannot, because **a written refusal is a list and a list needs a head at the top of it, and there is a column on that wall with nothing at the head of it that nineteen hands declined to name and a vote in four minutes did not fill and did not rule out.** **Teodor Lask, nineteen, reads the refusal out loud twice at the ordinary speed. The two men it belongs to stand eleven feet apart in a shed that holds twenty-two and say nothing to each other.** Duss says thank you and it was not for the boy.
- **Nothing in this chapter is a fix.** No refusal is recorded, no line is entered, no heading is proposed, the fifth line stays empty, the sixth line stays empty for ever, the flat board stays under the trestle, the certified duplicate stays in its flat case, and the four names from Tace's form are not on the wall list and the wall list is not on the form.

### Kade's paper, the silence, and the room that cannot act (Ch 125)

- **Kade's confession is on the wall list in the Gate shed**, in her own hand, with her name and her day at the foot — **not a paper in the uplands**, and this is the Volume 02 fact re-stated on the page. **Marek is the reader that day.** A line is read twice at the ordinary speed and the room does not talk over a line. **The chapter claims no ordinal position for it.** The yard has three referents for a fourth line — the form's own line for anything anybody else has to say (Ch 87), the empty fourth line of a refused class counterweight (Ch 108), and this — and the batch does not add a fourth, and the collision is flagged in continuity rather than resolved here because resolving it means editing Ch 97 and Ch 108, which this phase does not own.
- **The chapter's finding is that the silence after the second reading is a different silence from the silence after the first.** The first is four hundred people hearing a thing for the first time and it goes past them like weather. The second is a room that has heard the same sentence on almost every morning since the register opened and has stopped finding it remote. **Nine or ten of the twenty people in that shed are on the fourth column of a Crown March form.**
- **The only piece of paper in this basin on which a counterweight has a number on it is the Hartlow roll in a loft, and the number is two** — two people on that paper, one of them nineteen, and **the nineteen-year-old is not one of the six on the roll.** The roll is six *lines*: four women over fifty including Ruta Vrabec, one a child, and the sixth a season examiner's name who is not a member and does not live there (Ch 102; corrected against Ch 97's looser "six women", and see the batch-0002 note that there is no sixth woman). **Tomek is the boy who comes after the four women die and is not a line on it (Ch 97).** Marek has known the number since the tenth month of MR 413, has checked it twice, and has wished in the plainest terms he is capable of anything that it were two for all four terraces. **CORRECTED at the third repair pass: this entry and `current.md` said "two people on a roll of six women, one of them nineteen", which put Tomek on the roll and reinstated "six women" after Ch 102 had corrected it.**
- **The cost of the chapter is an omission.** He does not go up the road, for the first time since the seventh, and the reader is not told yet that it is a failure. **He goes up the road in Ch 129 and tells Tomas everything, four minutes, in order, in a lane, before.** The Ch 125 omission is the flaw arriving one chapter early and is not redeemed by the Ch 129 confession.
- **The Hartlow number of two is Movement IV's raw material** and is the only countable counterweight in the volume. Movement IV may use it and may not turn it into a number for the other three terraces.

### Kade and Marek, counted (Ch 126)

- **One scene in this batch, and it is the only one in Movement III.** No first names, no physical affection, no warmth, nobody thanked, no plan built, nothing agreed. **She says both halves before he does and neither of them is wrong**: *you have done the one thing in this basin that there is no form for, and the class was the only honest thing on that paper* — and *there is no office in this basin that can send a person up a road to ask a household whether it can spare anything, and you are the only person in this basin who has ever tried to fix anything of this shape at all.* **The whole of Marek's defence is seven words long — the paper does not choose, I chose — and he has had nine days to find an eighth and there is not one.**
- **The romance count across the volume now stands at five co-presences (Chs 102, 108, 118, 119, 126) against the outline's guide of two.** The overage was already recorded after batch 0002 and this batch added one. **Volume 04 still owns the first honest disagreement about who should bear a risk and the shared practical method, and this batch takes neither**: nothing is built in Ch 126, no method is shared, and the only thing agreed is that she will be at the low ground on the twenty-second.
- **The harbour letter is used and not used (Ch 126). Thirteen weeks** paid for, sealed, on the seventh of the second month, on a shelf above a chandler's shop for the tide of the ninth, and about six weeks puts a letter in somebody's hand, so the answer was due in the third month and by the seventeenth of the growth month it had not come. **He works out that not one thing between that night and the fourth month turns on the answer, and does not go and look.** It is not opened, not paid for twice, and not a cheap way out. **It can be paid at any time in Movements IV or V and must not be a way out of a chapter.** **CORRECTED at the third repair pass: this entry said eleven weeks and batch 0004's prompt says the letter is eleven weeks old. Ch 126 and `open-threads.md` both say thirteen, the chapter is authoritative, and the prompt has been corrected to match. There is one letter and it is thirteen weeks old.**

### Sera Holt, and the mark that is shown once (Ch 127)

- **Sera Holt, fifty-two, chief physician of the Morrowfen clinic, a former senior witness, twenty-six years, enters for the first time since Ch 90 and is sent for by Mirren Fisk, and comes to the Elmsworth seed house in the low ground, which she has never once been in.** She gave the reason at the door in one sentence and did not give it twice: she has signed things about sixty-one women for two years without ever having seen the bench, and that is not a thing a physician is entitled to do.
- **She does not take his wrist.** She asks him what he did the day before yesterday and then the day before that. *You have not had a hole. That is worse.* A hole is a payment; **what he has is a season with a person still in it.**
- **She gives him one of five things and keeps four.** She can see that it is **not his own**; she will not say what it is, where it came from, **whose it is**, how much there is, or whether it comes down. **She has seen something with this shape before and will not say how many times, or whether any of them is standing in a room somewhere, and says she will answer all three the same way in nine years' time.**
- **The mark is written once and taken away in her bag.** One line and a date on a clinic sheet, and at the left-hand margin opposite the line a small hooked stroke with a short bar under it — **a character that is not in any of the four books a physician keeps, invented three times that night and thrown away twice.** **It is not given to him. It is not in the book, because a book would make it a thing with a history and it has no history, it has a body. It is not on his forearm, it is on a sheet, and no second person in this volume decodes it and no chapter of the series may.**
- **Her refusal is the volume's own rule turned on its protagonist:** no office in this kingdom is going to say out loud that a season may be given to a person by a man who has not asked that person, and not one of them is a coward. And she closes on the **fifth line**: about nine months of a season of a woman's hands are in him, the only place that could have said so in a form of its own is the fifth line of a form in a shed with a bench in it, **the yard is right, the yard is right for a reason that has nothing to do with him, and she is not going to be the third voice that fills it.** **The fifth line is not filled and the sixth line is empty for ever.**
- **Marek's forearm is not described, referred to, or looked at anywhere in Chapters 121–130.** The word does not appear. Holt's hand goes on the edge of a bench and on the rail of a chair. The year scar and the house book's struck line are Volume 16's and are not touched.

### The movement's turn, and what it costs the next two movements (Ch 130)

- **The terrible harvest is in the basin and the good harvest is in the terraces, and neither of them is where the paper said it would be.** Four hundred and some acres in the top corner of the region, four terraces inside **one** line of forty-one, and **no form in this kingdom in which a terrace is a line** — so one line has to carry a district that has come in short and four terraces that have come in well, and **there is nowhere on the sheet to put the second thing.**
- **Petrus Lenge's position, unchanged and restated by him first.** A book with one wrong number in it can be corrected in four seconds with two initials; **a book with a hole in it is a book that says out loud that the office cannot do its job.** He writes the true figure in beside the ten-year figure with his initials and **leaves the ten-year figure on the page** — two figures on one line is not a mistake — rules the foot with no total, and puts the slate on top of the bound book. **Neither figure can be used and neither can leave the room.**
- **The advance, restated once and in Lenge's mouth only:** eleven thousand and some hundreds, already in the ground, **in the best of the four terraces, put there in the second month of last year by men doing their jobs.** The exact figure is still not fixed in any mouth and was fixed nowhere in this batch.
- **The set-off stands.** Served the sixth of the third month at a holding gate, on a nail in a seed house, read by forty people in a day, and **the store it was going to empty is not going to be emptied, and nobody in that family knows.** This is Movement V's pool and it is now also Movement IV's problem: the set-off is against the *next* account, and the next account is a district figure that a district will answer with a rate.
- **The rate, going into Movement V:** by the fourth month a district will have a rate on it, and a rate goes on a person with a name, and there are about nine names available. **One of them is a haulier who paid four shillings for a mark, cannot get a line in about it, and is going to be in a room in the hills in about five weeks.**

### Tomas, in this batch (Chs 123, 128, 129)

- **Ch 123: he does not ask, and he makes Marek say whether anybody's name is on it.** Marek says no and then twice in one answer tells him whose *words* are in it. **Tomas: *that is the fourth time this year and it is the first one that cost somebody else something instead of you.*** He also says out loud, in a lane, that the second terrace's wall is not going to be right and he has told nobody and is telling Marek because if it went on a paper it would go up a road in nine months in somebody else's hand with a head at the top of it. **Marek does not write it down. That was correct.**
- **Ch 128: he builds a thing across the channel in nine hours to get a bar to the field, and says nothing about it**, and he will not speak of the three days to anybody, and Marek understands halfway through the third night that he is going to have to tell his brother, and that there is no form on which to do it, and that the only place left in this basin to say a thing like that is a lane with nobody in it, and that his brother will be on a road in about nine hours with a case in his hand.
- **Ch 129: the three things.** (1) The wall in about nine years and the weep in it, which has to stay in a margin on a board in a loft with no date on it, **because a paper outlives the men who are on it.** (2) The fourth-terrace store is not open, the key is in a coat, and **a man with a key in his hand in a valley with no form in it has no answer and Tomas does not expect one.** (3) Line four: eleven stakes, a rope, a culvert four feet below its invert, and in about nine years a bad winter somebody will decide about the road — *if there is a board in a loft then I will be forty-three and I will be standing on the road; if instead there is a form in a building a quarter of a mile from the ground then in nine years a man will read it and decide about the road from a desk, and there will be no ninth-year anybody to correct him.* He asks Marek to say the word **right** so he can tell him it was not there, **and it is not there.** He is not forgiven, he is not a hero, he is not thanked, and the volume's resolution does not make him safe.
- **Lio is not in this batch and nobody has written on her slate.**

### Janko Vrabec, in this batch (Ch 128)

- **He comes up eleven switchbacks with a bar in the growth month for no reason anybody could put on a works, and nobody asks why.** On the second night he says his whole speech, which is about the ninth line of the works, the key, and **the fact that neither of them has asked the other, twice noticed**, and that the ninth line is empty and will stay empty until the winter and that after the winter he is not going to be there to fill it. **He is not a party to anything, is not the story, and no chapter of this batch turns him into a symbol.** Ch 135 is his chapter and this batch only plants him.
- **The key is not given, not asked for, and does not change hands. Marek does not enter the ninth line and does not propose a keyholder.** The ninth line is still empty and waiting.

### Third terrace, in this batch (Ch 128)

- **Four people live on it and none of them is named in Chapters 121–130, and none of them is asked anything, and no name from the third terrace enters this batch.** Its cutting was done by nine men who do not live on it, on the second to the fifth of the harvest month, with a bar's end on a thing Tomas Vale built, while Marek held a crossed boundary in a channel. **There is no form in this kingdom in which a cutting can be entered as having been done, and no name on the board, and no office that could be told.** Movement IV's Ch 134 owns the third terrace's being unreached and must not find a name here.

### Budgets and guardrails, verified from the files after the fix pass

- **Length, counted with `wc -w` from the files with the title line included, after the THIRD fix pass: 25,544 words across ten chapters, inside the 24,000–28,000 plan.** Distribution: Ch 121 2,756 · Ch 122 2,502 · Ch 123 2,278 · Ch 124 2,435 · Ch 125 2,009 · Ch 126 2,714 · Ch 127 2,539 · Ch 128 2,441 · Ch 129 3,495 · Ch 130 2,375. Shortest 2,009, longest 3,495. **This is the first Volume 03 batch inside the plan; batch 0001 came in at 30,139 and batch 0002 at 25,606.** **The third pass moved the total by nine words. The review log for this phase is truncated before its verdict and the 27,868 it appears to compute is the sum of eleven files and wrongly includes Ch 120; a reader should not treat it as a discrepancy with this line.**
- **Kalendar is clean after the fix pass.** A case-insensitive sweep for the twelve real-world month names and the seven weekdays was run and **returned seven blocking hits, all real weekdays, all fixed: *Thursday* and *Monday* and *Friday* in Ch 124 (a future day, a weekly reading habit, a day of the week twice, and a weekly market habit), *Thursday* and *Friday* in Ch 125, and three instances of *Thursday* in Ch 127** (two in Holt's speech and one in the paragraph that explains why she said it). All were replaced with ordinal or rota language. **The re-sweep returns nothing.** Every *March* in the batch is *Crown March*, which is a street in Bell Stair, a district and a region, and which batch 0004's prompt already tells the next writer to exclude before grepping. *May* appears only as the lowercase verb. **The growth month's twenty-day length is not restated, no other month's length is stated, no event is placed after the nineteenth of the growth month, and no days are counted backwards across a month boundary.** Harvest-month dates run second to eleventh. **One construction was malformed rather than a real-world name and is fixed at the third pass: Ch 128 opened *in the second month of the growth month*, nesting an ordinal month inside a seasonal one, and now reads *stood in the second month and did not go down through the growth month*.**
- **Panel budget: ZERO Ledger Sight panels in ten chapters.** `grep -c "^>"` returns 0 in every file. **The words *Ledger Sight*, *standing hold* and *Boundary Hold* do not appear anywhere in Chapters 121–130**, and the founding standing hold is not used, offered, wished for or hinted at. **Season Hand is not named in the prose and is never used before Ch 121.** The power change is carried entirely by hands, rooms, fields and costs and not by a panel or a notification.
- **No chapter's load-bearing event is a document read out to a room.** Ch 125 *is* a reading, and it is carried by the rota, the chalk, the counter, a queue, a woman with a rope, and the question that breaks the silence; Ch 128 is a channel and nine men with scythes; Ch 129 is a bar on a wall and a case and a chalk that goes down. **There is no copy-room scene and no corridor in any of the ten chapters**, and no chapter opens with a date and a feeling.
- **No villain, no new faction, no conviction, no dissolution, no closed office.** Belic is right about trays. Fisk refuses to answer Rell's letter twice and answers a third time. Teran refuses to name a class twice before she names a household. Holt refuses five times. Lenge is right about a column and says the good part about the four terraces is correct. Kade accuses and defends in the same breath. Vukic is wrong about the drain and wrong not to say. Droll makes a new call and says she does not know that it is right. **Nothing was fixed, resolved, entered, recorded, named, or made permanent.**
- **No figure exists anywhere for the price of barley and none may be invented.** The only figures about the harvest are *about a third in nine days* and *the rate has not moved*. The four shillings in Ch 129 is the cost of a mark, established in Ch 101. **The advance appears once, in Lenge's mouth, as eleven thousand and some hundreds, and is not fixed.** **No locked figure of Volume 02 has been changed and none appears outside a Tallow scene**; none of 640, 440, 279h 40p, 441, 160 lawful hundreds of pints, sixty pints in nine days, or 240 and 580 per cent appears in this batch.
- **No chapter heading is proposed for any bare column.** The wall list's fourth column, the rota, the bench book, the exercise book, the chaff-seller's list and the fifth line of the form all stay bare. **Sanne Droll says out loud in Ch 129 that a written refusal is a list and a list needs a head, and that is the reason the refusal is not written down** — which is the batch's answer to its own temptation and not a fix.
- **Romance: one Kade-and-Marek scene in this batch (Ch 126)**, against a volume guide of two and a running count of five. Recorded, not concealed. No first names, no warmth, nobody thanked, nothing built, no method shared.
- **Every verification sentence above was produced by writing the ten chapters, running a repair pass, and grepping the files.** A reviewer should re-read before treating any of it as closed.

### Second review-repair pass — fourteen further findings, all fixed, and two items that are NOT the writer's to fix

- **Fixed.** Ch 128's hold was internally inconsistent four ways and, worse, could not physically cover the cutting it exists for; it is now one continuous hold of two and a half days with a single water clock that reconciles at three days and four hours. Ch 127 was in the wrong half of the map (the Elmsworth seed house is in Rainmarket, and Sera Holt was made to climb a road to reach a room in her own city) and is corrected. Ch 129 gave Tomas a son of twenty-eight where Lio is a daughter of nine. Ch 129 dated the Ch 109 kitchen to the ninth month of last year and the wait to ten months where both are the second month of MR 414 and five months. Ch 130 dated the Ch 118 depot scene to the growth month and split one event in two. Ch 128 said he would never be able to tell his brother and Ch 129 undid it the next day. Ch 124's Red Uplands was a hundred and forty acres against four hundred in six other chapters. Ch 125's reading frequency and start date put the line's first hearing before the register existed. Ch 129 had forty people in a shed that holds twenty-two. Ch 126 called a seven-word sentence nine words. Ch 126 had the harbour letter still on its shelf eleven weeks after the post was due to have taken it. Ch 123's emphasis swallowed a sentence break; Ch 121 had a grammar slip and no explanation of why a low-ground tray was in scope; Ch 122 had a stray *nine words*; Ch 124 had a day-count that did not reconcile; Ch 129 had Duss carrying a paper for four months when it is about eleven; Ch 130's closer was a 250-word restatement of five beats and four of them appeared twice in the same chapter; Ch 130's straw figure, Ch 124's *four days of a rate*, and Ch 130's *about nine* density were corrected; and the closers of Chs 121, 122, 125, 127 and 129 were reworded so that the register paragraph is not the ending of six of the ten chapters. **No chapter was restarted, no card was moved, and no planned plot changed.**
- **The one that mattered.** The batch had defined the first direction's cost twice — as his own habits in Ch 123 and as a stranger's hands in Ch 126 — and dated both to the ninth of the growth month. **They are two costs and Ch 126 now says so in Marek's own head: on the ninth what he lost was his own boiler, his own door, his own ledger, and that was a bill; on the seventeenth he came out of a ditch holding the use of somebody else's working life, and that is not a bill, and nobody charged him for it and nobody could.**
- **NOT THE WRITER'S TO FIX — the outline owner should decide.** `outline/volume-03.md` says *two scenes with Kade and Marek in this volume, at most.* The running count is **five** (Chs 102, 108, 118, 119, 126) and batch 0003 added the fifth. It is recorded here and in `state/current.md` rather than concealed, and batch 0004's prompt caps Movement IV at one further co-presence, so the volume will end at six at most. **Ch 126 stays inside the tone limit — no first names, no warmth, no thanks, nothing built, no method shared — so the breach is the count and not the writing.** Either the outline's number is raised, or Movements IV and V need an explicit instruction to keep her off the page, and that is a decision about the volume's shape rather than about a chapter.
- **NOT THE WRITER'S TO FIX — a pre-existing collision, flagged not resolved.** *A fourth line* has three referents in this volume: the form's own fourth line, which is for anything anybody else has to say (Ch 87); the empty fourth line of a refused class counterweight, read out twice as empty (Ch 108, Ch 110); and Kade's sentence, which Ch 97 puts on the fourth line and on the same sheet as the entry. **Ch 125 therefore claims no ordinal position for Kade's line and refers to it as *the one that was hers*, which is a deliberate avoidance and is the reason.** Resolving it properly means reading Ch 97 and Ch 108 against each other and possibly editing them, and this phase does not own either. A reviewer should treat *a fourth line* as ambiguous throughout Volume 02 and Volume 03 and not quote it back at a writer as a fixed position.


### Third review-repair pass — four findings, all fixed, and four checks that came back clean

**The review log for this phase, `logs/next-0002.review.log`, is a truncated agent transcript. It ends mid-inspection, before any verdict, so it contains no findings list. What it does contain is the set of checks it was running, and every one of those checks was carried to completion here. The four items below came out of re-reading the ten chapters against the chapters that own each fact.**

- **1. Blocking, and the batch contradicted itself in three places: who holds the second-terrace store with a lock.** **Ch 122 and Ch 124 both say Stana Peric has no store of her own and that the holder is her mother** — fifty-eight, whom nobody in four terraces counts, which is the compliment Ch 124 is built on and the reason Marek understands he spent a season to save a store somebody was going to empty anyway. **Ch 113 is the owning chapter: the eighth line is the second terrace's store and it has a name on it because a store room with a lock has a holder; the ninth is the fourth-terrace store and the only one with a key.** Ch 124's closing beat, Ch 125's closer and Ch 130's good-harvest paragraph all said she held it. **The mother holds it.** Three chapter sentences corrected, and the same error corrected here, in `current.md`, in `chapter-summaries.md` and in `open-threads.md`, all four of which had inherited it from the draft. **The ninth line is unaffected: no keyholder, Janko Vrabec cut his own name out of it in the second month, and he is going to be dead in the winter.**
- **2. Ch 125 put the nineteen-year-old on the Hartlow roll.** Ch 97 is explicit that Tomek is the boy who comes *after* the four women die and is not a line on that roll, and Ch 102 corrects the roll's composition to six *lines* — four women over fifty, one a child, and the sixth an examiner's name who is not a member and does not live there. The first repair pass had already caught "two women" here and half-fixed it into a phrase that reinstated "six women" *and* put Tomek on the roll; **the sentence now reads that there are two people on that paper, one of them is nineteen, and the nineteen-year-old is not one of the six on the roll.** `current.md` and this file carried the same wrong phrase and are corrected.
- **3. Ch 128 opened on a malformed calendar construction.** *In the second month of the growth month* nests an ordinal month inside a seasonal one, which is not a form this calendar has anywhere else in fifty chapters. **It now reads that the water stood in the second month and did not go down through the growth month.** No date, month length, or event placement changed.
- **4. The harbour letter was two ages in two files.** Ch 126 and `open-threads.md` say thirteen weeks; this file and batch 0004's prompt said eleven. The chapter is authoritative, the two agreeing files were stale from the second pass, and **the prompt has been corrected to thirteen so the next writer is not handed a figure the chapter contradicts.**
- **Checked and deliberately NOT changed, recorded so the work is not repeated.** **(a)** Every real-world-month hit in the batch is *Crown March*, which is a street in Bell Stair, a district and a region; batch 0004's prompt already warns to exclude it before grepping, and the earlier sweep was correct. **(b)** The absence of *Ledger Sight*, *Boundary Hold* and *Season Hand* from the prose is a deliberate recorded choice — the volume's panel budget is zero — and is not a missing name. **(c)** The batch's recorded 25,535 word count was correct; the 27,868 in the review log is the sum of eleven files and includes Ch 120, which is not this batch's. **(d)** There are no duplicated or near-duplicated paragraphs anywhere in the ten chapters, checked on full-block and on first-eight-words keys.
- **No chapter was restarted, no card was moved, no planned plot changed, and no figure in the batch moved except the nine words the corrections themselves cost.**
